# Minimizers of one-dimensional regularization problems with linear and nonlinear total variation

**arXiv ID**: 2608.00604v1
**Authors**: Luca Lussardi, Marco Morandotti, Federico Stra
**Published**: 2026-08-01
**Categories**: math.CA, math.OC
**Comments**: 48 pages, 7 figures
**HTML URL**: https://arxiv.org/html/2608.00604v1

## Abstract

A study of the minimizers of one-dimensional Rudin-Osher-Fatemi-type functionals with linear or nonlinear total variation and fidelity term is undertaken. Conditions on the input datum and the parameters of the functional are found that force the input itself to be the minimizer or not. In the linear setting the discriminating conditions on the parameters are complementary highlighting the sharpness of the results. The nonlinear setting is substantially different: while the non-minimality of the datum is treated in analogy with the linear case, for the minimality of the input only partial answers are found. The results in the nonlinear setting hinge on auxiliary constrained or penalized minimization problems investigating the behavior of optimal transitions with prescribed height. Additionally, they are complemented by some numerical examples.

## Full Text

Minimizers of one-dimensional regularization problems with linear and nonlinear total variation

## Minimizers of one-dimensional regularization problems
with linear and nonlinear total variationLuca LussardiPolitecnico di Torino, Torino,luca.lussardi@polito.it,orcid:0000-0001-9130-3573Marco MorandottiPolitecnico di Torino, Torino,marco.morandotti@polito.it,orcid:0000-0003-3528-6152Federico StraPolitecnico di Torino, Torino,federico.stra@polito.it,orcid:0000-0001-8151-1045

## Abstract

A study of the minimizers of one-dimensional Rudin–Osher–Fatemi-type functionals with linear or
nonlinear total variation and fidelity term is undertaken.
Conditions on the input datum and the parameters of the functional are found that force the
input itself to be the minimizer or not.
In the linear setting the discriminating conditions on the parameters are complementary
highlighting the sharpness of the results.
The nonlinear setting is substantially different:
while the non-minimality of the datum is treated in analogy with the linear case,
for the minimality of the input only partial answers are found.
The results in the nonlinear setting hinge on auxiliary constrained or penalized minimization problems
investigating the behavior of optimal transitions with prescribed height.
Additionally, they are complemented by some numerical examples.

energy minimization, (nonlinear) total variation, regularity of minimizers, optimality conditions.

## 1Introduction

In the framework of image processing, the total variation energy functional plays a fundamental role. Since the pioneering work of Rudin, Osher, and Fatemi[11], total variation regularization has been extensively studied both from the theoretical and applied viewpoints, leading to a wide literature on qualitative properties of minimizers and on nonlinear variants of the classical model (see the survey[6]and the references therein; in particular, see also[7]and[8]).

Given an intervalI⊆ℝI\subseteq\mathbb{R}, a datumg:I→ℝg:I\to\mathbb{R}describing the gray level of an input image,λ,γ>0\lambda,\gamma>0, andp∈[1,∞]p\in[1,\infty], we study one-dimensional minimization problems for functionals of the formF​(u)=TV⁡(u;I)+λ​∥u−g∥pγF(u)=\operatorname{TV}(u;I)+\lambda\lVert u-g\rVert_{p}^{\gamma}

as well as their nonlinear counterpart obtained by replacing the classical total variationTV\operatorname{TV}with a generalized functionalTVφ,θ\operatorname{TV}_{\varphi,\theta}. Precisely, inSubsection 2.1we defineTV⁡(u;I)\operatorname{TV}(u;I)as thepointwiseversion of the total variation of the functionuuover the setII. Up to a choice of good representatives, this corresponds to the quantity|D​u|​(I)\lvert\mathop{}\!\mathrm{D}u\rvert(I)defined foru∈BV⁡(I)u\in\operatorname{BV}(I)according to[2]. Foru∈BV⁡(I)u\in\operatorname{BV}(I), we define also the nonlinear total variation asTVφ,θ⁡(u;I)=∫Iφ​(|u′|​(x))​d​x+|Dc​u|​(I)+∑x∈Juθ​(|[u]​(x)|),\operatorname{TV}\!\!_{\varphi,\theta}(u;I)=\int_{I}\varphi\bigl(\lvert u^{\prime}\rvert(x)\bigr)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x+\lvert\mathop{}\!\mathrm{D}^{c}u\rvert(I)+\sum_{x\in J_{u}}\theta\bigl(\lvert[u](x)\rvert\bigr),

whereφ,θ:[0,∞)→[0,∞)\varphi,\theta:[0,\infty)\to[0,\infty); seeSubsection 2.1for the precise definition, including the requirements onφ\varphiandθ\theta.

Among the relevant questions concerning this optimization problem, we are motivated by understanding
the regularity of the minimizers and the degeneracy of the problem.
In particular, in this article we address these questions:
- Q1:

Can the minimizer exhibit a Cantor partDc​u≠0\mathop{}\!\mathrm{D}^{c}u\neq 0?
- Q2:

Is the minimizeru=gu=g?

We will obtain and answer to the former question as a byproduct of our investigation of the latter.
With regard to Q2, our analysis shows that the behavior ofggnear the boundary of the domain is crucial.
More specifically, under the assumption of at least or at mostα\alpha-growth at the endpoints (seeSubsection 2.1), we determine conditions on the parametersp,γp,\gammawhich cause the inputggto be a minimizer for sufficiently largeλ\lambda, or cause it not to be a minimizer for anyλ\lambda.
The threshold1/γ≶1+1/(p​α)1/\gamma\lessgtr 1+1/(p\alpha)that we find for the two behaviors is sharp.

The linear case turns out to admit a more detailed analysis. We obtain several sharp results characterizing both the failure and the validity of minimality of the datum. On the one hand, we prove that functions exhibiting sufficiently fast growth near the boundary cannot be minimizers. On the other hand, monotone or piecewise monotone data with controlled growth at the endpoints are shown to be essentially unique minimizers for sufficiently large values of the fidelity parameterλ\lambda.
Moreover, the Cantor staircase function provides an interesting example showcasing that the minimizers may exhibit only the Cantor part of the derivative, thus answering Q1 positively.

By contrast, the nonlinear setting is considerably more difficult and remains only partially understood. Although we are able to establish some preliminary structural results and derive partial analogues of the linear theory, the nonlinear nature ofTVφ,θ\operatorname{TV}\!\!_{\varphi,\theta}leads to significant differences and obstacles with respect to the linear problem; as a consequence, most of the techniques developed in the linear case do not extend directly to the nonlinear case, mostly due to the fact that the functional exhibits a worse behavior with respect to local truncations. To work around this issue, we resort to the auxiliary boundary value problem (4.1), which allows us to characterize optimal transitions with prescribed height. Moreover, the failure of the analogues to the linear theorems is highlighted by the behavior of the minimizers of the penalized functional (4.7). Yet, our treatment of the nonlinear problem is only partial and we regard this part of the paper as mainly exploratory, with some examples suggesting that the nonlinear theory may exhibit genuinely different phenomena. This section is complemented by numerical examples to showcase and visualize some possible behaviors of the minimizers of (4.7).

Our proofs are mainly based on fine constructions of competitors for the minimization problems
heavily exploiting the convexity of the functionals. In addition, we resort to a range of techniques
from calculus of variations and convex analysis, including a detailed study of Euler–Lagrange
equations (for the nonlinear problem), the notion of perspective of a convex function, Legendre
transform, and other inequalities inspired by optimal transport on the real line.

The article is organized as follows. InSection 2we collect the necessary
preliminaries on functions of bounded variation and introduce the nonlinear total variation
functional.Section 3contains the main results for the linear model, distinguishing
between situations where the datum fails to be a minimizer and situations where it is optimal. InSection 4we turn to the nonlinear setting, where we analyze the associated boundary
value problem, obtain partial results which leave some open questions, and showcase the behavior of
optimal profiles by means of numerical examples. The approach is based on insight about the
minimization problem developed in the appendixSubsection B.1.1.

## 1.1Main results and open questions

We summarize here the main results contained in the paper.
- •

Linear total variation. After the simple and illustrative exampleTheorem 3.1with the Cantor staircase function, inTheorem 3.4we show that ifgghas sufficiently fast growth near the endpoints andγ\gammais large enough thenggis not a minimizer. InSubsection 3.2we provide progressively more general results of minimality of the input datum: we start with the simple situation inTheorem 3.7whereggis constant near the endpoints; we move on to the case inTheorem 3.8whereggis monotone and has controlled growth; finally, with the help of the fundamentalSubsection 3.2, inTheorem 3.10we extend the result to piecewise monotone functions. The complementarity of the ranges for the exponentγ\gammainTheorem 3.4andTheorem 3.8demonstrates the sharpness of our results.
- •

Nonlinear total variation. InTheorem 4.9we prove the analogue ofTheorem 3.4under the assumption of a lower bound on the boundary value problem (4.1).Subsection 4.3is more exploratory: as it turns out surprisingly, the desired nonlinear statement corresponding toSubsection 3.2is false, a fact which we demonstrate with a very simple counterexample inSubsection 4.3. The culprit is the non minimality of constant functions for a functional penalizing jumps at the right endpoint of the interval, as stated inSubsection 4.3. We demonstrate the behavior of minimizers of this penalized problem with some numerical examples inSubsection 4.3.1.

We stress the fact that our study of the linear case (Section 3) provides more precise
statements than what could be derived from the nonlinear case (Section 4) by
specialising toφ=θ=Id\varphi=\theta=\mathop{}\!\mathrm{Id}. This is because theSubsection 2.1ofTV\operatorname{TV}is a pointwise
version of total variation which is sensitive to pointwise modifications of the function, in
contrast withTVφ,θ\operatorname{TV}\!\!_{\varphi,\theta}ofSubsection 2.1, which is invariant in theL1L^{1}equivalence classes.

Our partial treatment of the regularity issue leaves open some questions more advanced than Q1,
for instance whether there exists a minimizeru∈SBVu\in\operatorname{SBV}when the input isg∈SBVg\in\operatorname{SBV}.
This could have implications in regularization of images.

Finally, in the nonlinear setting, future research may help identify sufficient conditions for the
minimality of the input, potentially based on the behavior in0and at∞\inftyof the
non-linearitiesφ\varphiandθ\theta. While our results show that there are situations where
the input performs better that certain specific competitors (seeSubsection 4.3),
the exposition inSubsection 4.3demonstrates that the situation is substantially more
involved than the linear case and a more detailed analysis is required to fully resolve this matter.

## 2Preliminaries

## 2.1Definitions and basic properties ofBV\operatorname{BV}functions

## Definition 2.1.

Given a subsetI⊆ℝI\subseteq\mathbb{R}and a functionu:I→ℝu:I\to\mathbb{R}, thetotal variationofuuoverIIisTV⁡(u;I)=sup{∑i=1n|u​(xi)−u​(xi−1)|:xi∈I​∀i∈{0,…,n}∧xi−1≤xi​∀i∈{1,…,n}}.\operatorname{TV}(u;I)=\sup\left\{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\lvert u(x_{i})-u(x_{i-1})\rvert:x_{i}\in I\ \forall i\in\{0,\dots,n\}\wedge x_{i-1}\leq x_{i}\ \forall i\in\{1,\dots,n\}\right\}.

The vector space offunctions with finite total variationoverIIisTV⁡(I)={u:I→ℝ:TV⁡(u;I)<∞}.\operatorname{TV}(I)=\{u:I\to\mathbb{R}:\operatorname{TV}(u;I)<\infty\}.

In the sequel, we may use the abbreviated notationTV⁡(u)\operatorname{TV}(u)instead ofTV⁡(u;I)\operatorname{TV}(u;I)when it is clear
from the context.

## Definition 2.2.

A functionu:I→ℝu:I\to\mathbb{R}is said to beregulatedif the left limitu−​(x)u^{-}(x)exists and is
finite at anyx∈ℝx\in\mathbb{R}which is an accumulation point ofIIfrom the left, and the right limitu−​(x)u^{-}(x)exists and is finite at anyx∈ℝx\in\mathbb{R}which is an accumulation point ofIIfrom the
right.

## Proposition 2.3.

Every functionu∈TV⁡(I)u\in\operatorname{TV}(I)is regulated.

## Definition 2.4.

A regulated functionu:I→ℝu:I\to\mathbb{R}is said to beregularifu​(x)∈[u−​(x)∧u+​(x),u−​(x)∨u+​(x)]u(x)\in[u^{-}(x)\wedge u^{+}(x),u^{-}(x)\vee u^{+}(x)]

for everyx∈Ix\in Iwhich is a bilateral accumulation point ofIIandu​(x)=u±​(x)u(x)=u^{\pm}(x)at any pointx∈Ix\in Iwhich is a unilateral accumulation point ofII.
The set ofregular functions with finite total variationsoverIIisTVreg⁡(I)={u∈TV⁡(I):uis regular}.\operatorname{TV}_{\operatorname{reg}}(I)=\{u\in\operatorname{TV}(I):\text{$u$ is regular}\}.

The total variationTV⁡(u;I)\operatorname{TV}(u;I)and the space of functionsTV⁡(I)\operatorname{TV}(I)are the pointwise version of|D​u|​(I)\lvert\mathop{}\!\mathrm{D}u\rvert(I)andBV⁡(I)\operatorname{BV}(I)as defined in[2]. More explicitly,TV⁡(u;I)\operatorname{TV}(u;I)is precisely
thepointwise variationdefined in[2, Definition 3.26];[2, Theorem 3.27]shows that the variation|D​u|​(I)\lvert\mathop{}\!\mathrm{D}u\rvert(I)is equal to the relaxation ofTV⁡(u,I)\operatorname{TV}(u,I)in theℒ1\mathscr{L}^{1}equivalence class (see also[2, (3.23)]);[2, Theorem 3.28]shows that any functionu∈BV⁡(I)u\in\operatorname{BV}(I)admits agood representativev∈TVreg⁡(I)v\in\operatorname{TV}_{\operatorname{reg}}(I)such that|D​u|​(I)=TV⁡(v;I)\lvert\mathop{}\!\mathrm{D}u\rvert(I)=\operatorname{TV}(v;I).

The following properties follow directly from the definitions.

LetI⊆ℝI\subseteq\mathbb{R}be an interval.
- (i)

Ifu∈TV⁡(I)u\in\operatorname{TV}(I), thenuuhas at most countably many discontinuities (and they are jumps).
- (ii)

Ifu∈TV⁡(I)u\in\operatorname{TV}(I), thenuuis regular if and only iflim infx′→xu​(x′)≤u​(x)≤lim supx′→xu​(x′)\liminf_{x^{\prime}\to x}u(x^{\prime})\leq u(x)\leq\limsup_{x^{\prime}\to x}u(x^{\prime})for every accumulation pointx∈Ix\in I.
- (iii)

Ifu,v∈TVreg⁡(I)u,v\in\operatorname{TV}_{\operatorname{reg}}(I)coincideℒ1\mathscr{L}^{1}-a.e., then they share the same set of jump points
and they coincide elsewhere.
- (iv)

Everyu∈BV⁡(I)u\in\operatorname{BV}(I)admits an equivalent functionv∈TVreg⁡(I)v\in\operatorname{TV}_{\operatorname{reg}}(I)which coincidesℒ1\mathscr{L}^{1}-a.e. withuu.
- (v)

Ifu∈TV⁡(I)u\in\operatorname{TV}(I), then a functionv:I→ℝv:I\to\mathbb{R}is regular and coincides withuuℒ1\mathscr{L}^{1}-a.e. if and only iflim infx′→xu​(x′)≤v​(x)≤lim supx′→xu​(x′)\liminf_{x^{\prime}\to x}u(x^{\prime})\leq v(x)\leq\limsup_{x^{\prime}\to x}u(x^{\prime})for everyx∈Ix\in I.
- (vi)

Ifuuis regulated andxxis a bilateral accumulation point ofII, thenu​(x)∈[u−​(x)∧u+​(x),u−​(x)∨u+​(x)]u(x)\in[u^{-}(x)\wedge u^{+}(x),u^{-}(x)\vee u^{+}(x)]if and only if the “pointwise variation”⟦u⟧=|u(x)−u−(x)|+|u+(x)−u(x)|\llbracket u\rrbracket=\lvert u(x)-u^{-}(x)\rvert+\lvert u^{+}(x)-u(x)\rvertis equal to the jump|[u]​(x)|=|u+​(x)−u−​(x)|\lvert[u](x)\rvert=\lvert u^{+}(x)-u^{-}(x)\rvert.

Regulated, regular andTV\operatorname{TV}functions are well behaved with regard to composition with continuous
or Lipschitz functions.

LetI⊆ℝI\subseteq\mathbb{R}.
- (i)

Ifu:I→ℝu:I\to\mathbb{R}is regulated andf∈C​(u​(I)¯)f\in C\bigl(\overline{u(I)}\bigr), thenf∘uf\circ uis
regulated.
- (ii)

Ifu:I→ℝu:I\to\mathbb{R}is regular andf∈C​(u​(I)¯)f\in C\bigl(\overline{u(I)}\bigr)is monotone, thenf∘uf\circ uis regular.
- (iii)

Ifu∈TV⁡(I)u\in\operatorname{TV}(I)andf∈Lip⁡(u​(I))f\in\operatorname{Lip}\bigl(u(I)\bigr), thenf∘u∈TV⁡(I)f\circ u\in\operatorname{TV}(I)andTV⁡(f∘u;I)≤Lip⁡(f)​TV⁡(u;I)\operatorname{TV}(f\circ u;I)\leq\operatorname{Lip}(f)\operatorname{TV}(u;I).

## Proof.

Since the rest are straightforward, we prove only(iii), which will play a crucial role
in what follows.
- (iii)

For every choice of sorted pointsx0<⋯<xnx_{0}<\dots<x_{n}inIIwe have∑i=1n|f​(u​(xi))−f​(u​(xi−1))|≤Lip⁡(f)​∑i=1n|u​(xi)−u​(xi−1)|≤Lip⁡(f)​TV⁡(u;I);\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\lvert f\bigl(u(x_{i})\bigr)-f\bigl(u(x_{i-1})\bigr)\right\rvert\leq\operatorname{Lip}(f)\sum_{i=1}^{n}\lvert u(x_{i})-u(x_{i-1})\rvert\leq\operatorname{Lip}(f)\operatorname{TV}(u;I);

taking the supremum on the left hand side proves the thesis. ∎

As a special application of the last two points ofSubsection 2.1we get the
following useful property.

## Remark 2.7.

Givenm,M∈ℝm,M\in\mathbb{R}, the functionsfm,fM,fmM∈Lip⁡(ℝ)f_{m},f^{M},f_{m}^{M}\in\operatorname{Lip}(\mathbb{R})defined asfm​(t)\displaystyle f_{m}(t)=t∨m,\displaystyle=t\vee m,fM​(t)\displaystyle f^{M}(t)=t∧M,\displaystyle=t\wedge M,fmM​(t)\displaystyle f_{m}^{M}(t)=t∨m∧M\displaystyle=t\vee m\wedge M

are11-Lipschitz and monotone, therefore ifu∈TV⁡(I)u\in\operatorname{TV}(I)then the truncated functionsu∨mu\vee m,u∧Mu\wedge Mandu∨m∧Mu\vee m\wedge Mare also inTV⁡(I)\operatorname{TV}(I)andTV⁡(u∨m;I),TV⁡(u∧M;I),TV⁡(u∨m∧M;I)≤TV⁡(u;I).\operatorname{TV}(u\vee m;I),\ \operatorname{TV}(u\wedge M;I),\ \operatorname{TV}(u\vee m\wedge M;I)\leq\operatorname{TV}(u;I).

Ifu∈TVreg⁡(I)u\in\operatorname{TV}_{\operatorname{reg}}(I), the truncated functions are also inTVreg⁡(I)\operatorname{TV}_{\operatorname{reg}}(I).

We collect here some further elementary properties of the pointwise total variation.

uucan be expressed as the difference of the two

ifu~:I→ℝ\tilde{u}:I\to\mathbb{R}is such thatlim infy→xu​(y)≤u~​(x)≤lim supy→xu​(y),\liminf_{y\to x}u(y)\leq\tilde{u}(x)\leq\limsup_{y\to x}u(y),

## Proof.

Property(i)is a well known fact;
the next two splitting properties are elementary;
therefore, we only prove(iv).
- (iv)

Letx0<x1<⋯<xnx_{0}<x_{1}<\dots<x_{n}be a sorted partition ofII. Givenε>0\varepsilon>0, for everyi∈{0,…,n}i\in\{0,\dots,n\}there exist distinct pointsxi,ε±∈Ix_{i,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\in Isuch thatu​(xi,ε−)−ε\displaystyle u(x_{i,\varepsilon}^{-})-\varepsilon≤u~​(xi)≤u​(xi,ε+)+ε\displaystyle\leq\tilde{u}(x_{i})\leq u(x_{i,\varepsilon}^{+})+\varepsilon∀i∈{0,…,n},\displaystyle\forall i\in\{0,\dots,n\},(2.1)xi−1,ε±\displaystyle x_{i-1,\varepsilon}^{\pm}<xi,ε±\displaystyle<x_{i,\varepsilon}^{\pm}∀i∈{1,…,n}.\displaystyle\forall i\in\{1,\dots,n\}.

For everyi∈{0,…,n}i\in\{0,\dots,n\}, letxi,ε′<xi,ε′′x_{i,\varepsilon}^{\prime}<x_{i,\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}such that{xi,ε′,xi,ε′′}={xi,ε−,xi,ε+}\{x_{i,\varepsilon}^{\prime},x_{i,\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}\}=\{x_{i,\varepsilon}^{-},x_{i,\varepsilon}^{+}\}.
The sequence of pointsx0,ε′<x0,ε′′<x1,ε′<⋯<xn,ε′<xn,ε′′x_{0,\varepsilon}^{\prime}<x_{0,\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}<x_{1,\varepsilon}^{\prime}<\dots<x_{n,\varepsilon}^{\prime}<x_{n,\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}forms
a partition ofII.
By the triangle inequality and (2.1) we get|u​(xi,ε′)−u~​(xi)|+|u~​(xi)−u​(xi,ε′′)|=|u​(xi,ε+)−u~​(xi)|+|u~​(xi)−u​(xi,ε−)|≤|(u​(xi,ε+)+ε)−u~​(xi)|+|u~​(xi)−(u​(xi,ε−)−ε)|+2​ε=|(u​(xi,ε+)+ε)−(u​(xi,ε−)−ε)|+2​ε≤|u​(xi,ε+)−u​(xi,ε−)|+4​ε=|u​(xi,ε′)−u​(xi,ε′′)|+4​ε,\begin{split}\lvert u(x_{i,\varepsilon}^{\prime})-\tilde{u}(x_{i})\rvert+\lvert\tilde{u}(x_{i})-u(x_{i,\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime})\rvert&=\lvert u(x_{i,\varepsilon}^{+})-\tilde{u}(x_{i})\rvert+\lvert\tilde{u}(x_{i})-u(x_{i,\varepsilon}^{-})\rvert\\
&\leq\left\lvert\bigl(u(x_{i,\varepsilon}^{+})+\varepsilon\bigr)-\tilde{u}(x_{i})\right\rvert+\left\lvert\tilde{u}(x_{i})-\bigl(u(x_{i,\varepsilon}^{-})-\varepsilon\bigr)\right\rvert+2\varepsilon\\
&=\left\lvert\bigl(u(x_{i,\varepsilon}^{+})+\varepsilon\bigr)-\bigl(u(x_{i,\varepsilon}^{-})-\varepsilon\bigr)\right\rvert+2\varepsilon\\
&\leq\lvert u(x_{i,\varepsilon}^{+})-u(x_{i,\varepsilon}^{-})\rvert+4\varepsilon=\lvert u(x_{i,\varepsilon}^{\prime})-u(x_{i,\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime})\rvert+4\varepsilon,\end{split}

from which we obtain∑i=1n|u~​(xi−1)−u~​(xi)|≤∑i=1n(|u~​(xi−1)−u​(xi−1,ε′′)|+|u​(xi−1,ε′′)−u​(xi,ε′)|+|u​(xi,ε′)−u~​(xi)|)≤∑i=0n(|u​(xi,ε′)−u~​(xi)|+|u~​(xi)−u​(xi,ε′′)|)+∑i=1n|u​(xi−1,ε′′)−u​(xi,ε′)|≤∑i=0n|u​(xi,ε′)−u​(xi,ε′′)|+∑i=1n|u​(xi−1,ε′′)−u​(xi,ε′)|+4​(n+1)​ε.\begin{split}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\lvert\tilde{u}(x_{i-1})-\tilde{u}(x_{i})\rvert&\leq\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\lvert\tilde{u}(x_{i-1})-u(x_{i-1,\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime})\rvert+\lvert u(x_{i-1,\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime})-u(x_{i,\varepsilon}^{\prime})\rvert+\lvert u(x_{i,\varepsilon}^{\prime})-\tilde{u}(x_{i})\rvert\right)\\
&\leq\sum_{i=0}^{n}\left(\lvert u(x_{i,\varepsilon}^{\prime})-\tilde{u}(x_{i})\rvert+\lvert\tilde{u}(x_{i})-u(x_{i,\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime})\rvert\right)+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\lvert u(x_{i-1,\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime})-u(x_{i,\varepsilon}^{\prime})\rvert\\
&\leq\sum_{i=0}^{n}\lvert u(x_{i,\varepsilon}^{\prime})-u(x_{i,\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime})\rvert+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\lvert u(x_{i-1,\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime})-u(x_{i,\varepsilon}^{\prime})\rvert+4(n+1)\varepsilon.\end{split}

By definition of total variation, the right hand side is less than or equal toTV⁡(u;I)+4​(n+1)​ε\operatorname{TV}(u;I)+4(n+1)\varepsilon.
Sendingε→0\varepsilon\to 0and taking the supremum of the left hand side over the partition(xi)0≤i≤n(x_{i})_{0\leq i\leq n}we deduce the thesis. ∎

We recall the classical decompositionD​u=Da​u+Dc​u+Dj​u\mathop{}\!\mathrm{D}u=\mathop{}\!\mathrm{D}^{a}u+\mathop{}\!\mathrm{D}^{c}u+\mathop{}\!\mathrm{D}^{j}uof the derivative of a functionu∈BV⁡(I)u\in\operatorname{BV}(I); see[2, Corollary 3.33]. The (linear) total variation of a functionu∈TVreg⁡(I)u\in\operatorname{TV}_{\operatorname{reg}}(I)can be represented asTV⁡(u;I)=∫I|u′​(x)|​d​x+|Dc​u|​(I)+∑x∈Ju|[u]​(x)|,\operatorname{TV}(u;I)=\int_{I}\lvert u^{\prime}(x)\rvert\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x+\lvert\mathop{}\!\mathrm{D}^{c}u\rvert(I)+\sum_{x\in J_{u}}\lvert[u](x)\rvert,

whereJuJ_{u}is the jump set ofuuand[u]​(x)=g+​(x)−g−​(x)[u](x)=g^{+}(x)-g^{-}(x)is the jump value.
This leads us to introduce the nonlinear total variationTVφ,θ⁡(u;I)\operatorname{TV}\!\!_{\varphi,\theta}(u;I)as follows.

## Definition 2.9.

Given two non-decreasing functionsφ,θ:[0,∞)→[0,∞)\varphi,\theta:[0,\infty)\to[0,\infty)such thatφ\varphiis convex,θ\thetais concave,φ​(0)=θ​(0)=0\varphi(0)=\theta(0)=0andlimt→∞φ​(t)t=limt→0+θ​(t)t=θ′​(0)=1,\lim_{t\to\infty}\frac{\varphi(t)}{t}=\lim_{t\to 0^{+}}\frac{\theta(t)}{t}=\theta^{\prime}(0)=1,

thenonlinear total variationofu∈TV⁡(I)u\in\operatorname{TV}(I)isTVφ,θ⁡(u;I)=∫Iφ​(|u′|​(x))​d​x+|Dc​u|​(I)+∑x∈Juθ​(|[u]​(x)|).\operatorname{TV}\!\!_{\varphi,\theta}(u;I)=\int_{I}\varphi\bigl(\lvert u^{\prime}\rvert(x)\bigr)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x+\lvert\mathop{}\!\mathrm{D}^{c}u\rvert(I)+\sum_{x\in J_{u}}\theta\bigl(\lvert[u](x)\rvert\bigr).

It is simple to verify that the following conditions are equivalent:φ​(t)=t,∀t∈[0,∞)\displaystyle\varphi(t)=t,\ \forall t\in[0,\infty)⇔\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow∃t∈(0,∞):φ​(t)=t\displaystyle\exists t\in(0,\infty):\varphi(t)=t⇔\displaystyle\Leftrightarrowφ′​(0)=1.\displaystyle\varphi^{\prime}(0)=1.

Our main theorems rely on the following property of the input datum near the endpoints.
The precise assumption will be anyway recalled explicitly in their statements.

## Definition 2.10.

Givenα>0\alpha>0, a functionu:(a,b)→ℝu:(a,b)\to\mathbb{R}which admits the limitg+​(a)g^{+}(a)is said to haveat mostα\alpha-growthat the endpointaaif there existsC>0C>0such that|g​(x)−g+​(a)|≤C​(x−a)α\lvert g(x)-g^{+}(a)\rvert\leq C(x-a)^{\alpha}forx∈(a,b)x\in(a,b). It is said to haveat leastα\alpha-growthif the reverse inequality holds.

## 2.2Technical results

## Definition 2.11.

Given a measurable functionu:Ω→[0,∞]u:\Omega\to[0,\infty],
letμu:[0,∞]→[0,|Ω|]\mu_{u}:[0,\infty]\to[0,\lvert\Omega\rvert]be itssuper-level set measureμu​(t)=|{x∈Ω:u​(x)>t}|.\mu_{u}(t)=\lvert\{x\in\Omega:u(x)>t\}\rvert.

This function is non-increasing and lower semi-continuous, therefore it admits a non-increasing
lower semi-continuous pseudo-inverse(μt)−1:[0,|Ω|]→[0,∞](\mu_{t})^{-1}:[0,\lvert\Omega\rvert]\to[0,\infty]given by(μu)−1​(s)=inf{t:μu​(t)≤s}.(\mu_{u})^{-1}(s)=\inf\{t:\mu_{u}(t)\leq s\}.

We define thenon-increasingandnon-decreasing rearrangementsu†,u†:[0,|Ω|]→[0,∞]u_{\dagger},u^{\dagger}:[0,\lvert\Omega\rvert]\to[0,\infty]ofuuasu†​(s)=(μu)−1​(s),u†​(s)=(μu)−1​(|Ω|−s),∀s∈[0,|Ω|].u_{\dagger}(s)=(\mu_{u})^{-1}(s),\qquad u^{\dagger}(s)=(\mu_{u})^{-1}(\lvert\Omega\rvert-s),\qquad\forall s\in[0,\lvert\Omega\rvert].

The functionsμu\mu_{u}and(μu)−1(\mu_{u})^{-1}are related by the crucial propertyμu​(t)≤s⟺(μu)−1​(s)≤t.\mu_{u}(t)\leq s\Longleftrightarrow(\mu_{u})^{-1}(s)\leq t.

Moreover, one can show thatμu†=μu†=μu\mu_{u_{\dagger}}=\mu_{u^{\dagger}}=\mu_{u}(seeSubsection 2.2,
where it is implicitly proven), therefore by the layer-cake representation we have∫Ωf​(u​(x))​d​x=∫0|Ω|f​(u†​(s))​d​s=∫0|Ω|f​(u†​(s))​d​s\int_{\Omega}f\bigl(u(x)\bigr)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x=\int_{0}^{\lvert\Omega\rvert}f\bigl(u_{\dagger}(s)\bigr)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}s=\int_{0}^{\lvert\Omega\rvert}f\bigl(u^{\dagger}(s)\bigr)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}s

for everyf:[0,∞]→[0,∞]f:[0,\infty]\to[0,\infty]measurable.

The following proposition collects two contractivity properties of the monotone rearrangement, both
with respect to theLpL^{p}norm and the (linear) total variation. The proof is based upon two
technical lemmas, which we postpone. See also[12]for a survey about rearrangements.

## Remark 2.13.

Inequality (2.2) in the previousSubsection 2.2is closely related to the theory of optimal transport on

&=\{s\geq 0:\mu_{u}(t)>s\}=\bigl[0,\mu_{u}(t)\bigr),\end{split}

thereforeμ​((t,∞))=ℒ1​(u−1​(t,∞))=ℒ1​({x∈(0,1):u​(x)>t})=μu​(t)\mu\bigl((t,\infty)\bigr)=\mathscr{L}^{1}\bigl(u^{-1}(t,\infty)\bigr)=\mathscr{L}^{1}\bigl(\{x\in(0,1):u(x)>t\}\bigr)=\mu_{u}(t)

and(u†)#​(ℒ1​¬(0,1))​((t,∞))=ℒ1​((u†)−1​((t,∞)))=ℒ1​([0,μu​(t)))=μu​(t).(u_{\dagger})_{\#}\bigl(\mathscr{L}^{1}\mathop{\raisebox{-0.5468pt}{\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin={br}]{-90.0}{$\lnot$}}}}(0,1)\bigr)\bigl((t,\infty)\bigr)=\mathscr{L}^{1}\bigl((u_{\dagger})^{-1}\bigl((t,\infty)\bigr)\bigr)=\mathscr{L}^{1}\bigl([0,\mu_{u}(t))\bigr)=\mu_{u}(t).

Define now the transport plansπ,π†∈Π​(μ,ν)\pi,\pi_{\dagger}\in\Pi(\mu,\nu)given byπ\displaystyle\pi=(u,v)#​(ℒ1​¬(0,1)),\displaystyle=(u,v)_{\#}\bigl(\mathscr{L}^{1}\mathop{\raisebox{-0.5468pt}{\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin={br}]{-90.0}{$\lnot$}}}}(0,1)\bigr),π†\displaystyle\pi_{\dagger}=(u†,v†)#​(ℒ1​¬(0,1)).\displaystyle=(u_{\dagger},v_{\dagger})_{\#}\bigl(\mathscr{L}^{1}\mathop{\raisebox{-0.5468pt}{\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin={br}]{-90.0}{$\lnot$}}}}(0,1)\bigr).

The planπ†\pi_{\dagger}is supported on a monotone graph111A setΓ⊂ℝ2\Gamma\subset\mathbb{R}^{2}such that(x2−x1)​(y2−y1)≥0(x_{2}-x_{1})(y_{2}-y_{1})\geq 0for every(x1,y1),(x1,y2)∈Γ(x_{1},y_{1}),(x_{1},y_{2})\in\Gamma.,
hence it iscc-cyclically monotone with respect to the costc​(x,y)=|x−y|pc(x,y)=\lvert x-y\rvert^{p},
therefore it is optimal

actually an equivalence; observe that their inequality (4.5) there is related to ourSubsection 2.2; see also[1, Theorem 1.11]for the
optimality of the monotone rearrangement). &\leq\int_{[0,\infty)^{2}}\lvert x-y\rvert^{p}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\pi(x,y)=\int_{0}^{1}\lvert u(z)-v(z)\rvert^{p}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}z=\lVert u-v\rVert_{p}^{p}.\end{split}

## Lemma 2.14.

Iff:ℝ→ℝf:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}is a convex function anda≤Aa\leq Aandb≤Bb\leq B, thenf​(b−a)+f​(B−A)≤f​(B−a)+f​(b−A).f(b-a)+f(B-A)\leq f(B-a)+f(b-A).

Moreover, the inequality is strict ifffis strictly convex,a<Aa<Aandb<Bb<B.

In particular, for everyp≥1p\geq 1we have|b−a|p+|B−A|p≤|B−a|p+|b−A|p\lvert b-a\rvert^{p}+\lvert B-A\rvert^{p}\leq\lvert B-a\rvert^{p}+\lvert b-A\rvert^{p}.
Ifp>1p>1the inequality is strict if and only ifa<Aa<A, andb<Bb<B.
Forp=1p=1, the inequality is strict if and only ifa<Aa<A,b<Bb<B,a<Ba<Bandb<Ab<A, which is
equivalent tomax⁡{a,b}<min⁡{A,B}\max\{a,b\}<\min\{A,B\}.

## Proof.

Letx≔b−A≤b−a≕Xx\coloneqq b-A\leq b-a\eqqcolon XandΔ​b=B−b≥0\Delta b=B-b\geq 0.
The convexity offfimplies the monotonicity of forward differences, therefore we havef​(x+Δ​b)−f​(x)≤f​(X+Δ​b)−f​(X),f(x+\Delta b)-f(x)\leq f(X+\Delta b)-f(X),

that isf​(B−A)−f​(b−A)≤f​(B−a)−f​(b−a),f(B-A)-f(b-A)\leq f(B-a)-f(b-a),

which is equivalent to the thesis.
The inequality is strict ifffis strictly convex,x<Xx<XandΔ​b>0\Delta b>0, which are the stated conditions.

The inequality forp≥1p\geq 1and the characterization of strict inequality forp>1p>1follow usingf​(t)=|t|pf(t)=\lvert t\rvert^{p}.

Let us turn to the characterization of strict inequality forp=1p=1,f​(t)=|t|f(t)=\lvert t\rvert.
Ifx=Xx=XorΔ​b=0\Delta b=0, then equality holds. Letx<X,Δ​b>0x<X,\Delta b>0.
By the monotonicity of difference quotients,f​(x+Δ​b)−f​(x)Δ​b≤f​(X+Δ​b)−f​(x)X+Δ​b−x≤f​(X+Δ​b)−f​(X)Δ​b,\frac{f(x+\Delta b)-f(x)}{\Delta b}\leq\frac{f(X+\Delta b)-f(x)}{X+\Delta b-x}\leq\frac{f(X+\Delta b)-f(X)}{\Delta b},

where equality is verified if and only ifffis affine in(x,X+Δ​b)(x,X+\Delta b),
Therefore, the strict inequality holds if and only if0∈(x,X+Δ)0\in(x,X+\Delta), which amounts to the
required property ona,A,b,Ba,A,b,B.
∎

The next lemma is a classical rearrangement inequality, whose proof can be found in any textbook
about optimal transport, in relation to thecc-cyclical monotonicity with respect to the cost|x−y|\lvert x-y\rvert,
see e.g.[1].

## Lemma 2.15.

Letn∈ℕ+n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}and leta=(ai)i=0na=(a_{i})_{i=0}^{n}be a finite sequence of real numbers.
For every permutationσ∈𝒫n\sigma\in\mathcal{P}_{n}we have∑i=1n|aσ​(i)−aσ​(i−1)|≥maxi⁡ai−mini⁡ai,\sum_{i=1}^{n}\lvert a_{\sigma(i)}-a_{\sigma(i-1)}\rvert\geq\max_{i}a_{i}-\min_{i}a_{i},

with equality if and only if(aσ​(i))i=0n(a_{\sigma(i)})_{i=0}^{n}is monotone.

## Proposition 2.16.

Letg∈BV⁡((a,b))g\in\operatorname{BV}\bigl((a,b)\bigr)and letλ,γ>0\lambda,\gamma>0,p∈(0,∞]p\in(0,\infty].
Ifu∈BV⁡((a,b))u\in\operatorname{BV}\bigl((a,b)\bigr)minimizes the functionalF​(u)=TV⁡(u)+λ​∥u−g∥pγF(u)=\operatorname{TV}(u)+\lambda\lVert u-g\rVert_{p}^{\gamma}

andm≤g≤Mm\leq g\leq Mthen alsom≤u≤Mm\leq u\leq M.

## Proof.

Letu~=u∨m∧M\tilde{u}=u\vee m\wedge M.
ThenTV⁡(u~)≤TV⁡(u)\operatorname{TV}(\tilde{u})\leq\operatorname{TV}(u)bySubsection 2.1, and∥u~−g∥p≤∥u−g∥p\lVert\tilde{u}-g\rVert_{p}\leq\lVert u-g\rVert_{p},
with this latter inequality being strict unlessm≤u≤Mm\leq u\leq M.
∎

## Proposition 2.17.

Letg∈BV⁡((a,b))g\in\operatorname{BV}\bigl((a,b)\bigr)be a non-decreasing function andλ,γ>0\lambda,\gamma>0,p∈[1,∞]p\in[1,\infty].
Ifu∈BV⁡((a,b))u\in\operatorname{BV}\bigl((a,b)\bigr)minimizes the functionalF​(u)=TV⁡(u)+λ​∥u−g∥pγF(u)=\operatorname{TV}(u)+\lambda\lVert u-g\rVert_{p}^{\gamma}

then it is non-decreasing.

## Proof.

Ifggis constant, then the only minimizer isu=gu=g, which achievesF​(u)=0F(u)=0.
Indeed, ifuuis any other constant function, then∥u−g∥p>0\lVert u-g\rVert_{p}>0; otherwise, ifuuis not
constant, thenTV⁡(u)>0\operatorname{TV}(u)>0.

We may now assume thatggis not constant; therefore, there existx1<x2x_{1}<x_{2}such thatg​(x1)<g​(x2)g(x_{1})<g(x_{2}).
Letu¯​(x)=u†​(x−a)\bar{u}(x)=u^{\dagger}(x-a)be the non-decreasing rearrangement ofuusuitably translated to
preserve the same domain of definition. Sinceggis already non-decreasing, we haveg​(x)=g†​(x−a)g(x)=g^{\dagger}(x-a).
By (2.2) and (2.3)
ofSubsection 2.2we have∥u¯−g∥Lp​((a,b))=∥u†−g†∥Lp​((0,b−a))≤∥u−g∥Lp​((a,b)),\displaystyle\lVert\bar{u}-g\rVert_{L^{p}((a,b))}=\lVert u^{\dagger}-g^{\dagger}\rVert_{L^{p}((0,b-a))}\leq\lVert u-g\rVert_{L^{p}((a,b))},TV⁡(u¯;(a,b))=TV⁡(u†;(0,b−a))≤TV⁡(u;(a,b)),\displaystyle\operatorname{TV}\bigl(\bar{u};(a,b)\bigr)=\operatorname{TV}\bigl(u^{\dagger};(0,b-a)\bigr)\leq\operatorname{TV}\bigl(u;(a,b)\bigr),

and both inequalities must be saturated.
By the equality conditions stated inSubsection 2.2,
the latter forcesuuto be monotone. We wish to prove thatuuis actually non-decreasing.
By contradiction, suppose there existx3<x4x_{3}<x_{4}such thatu​(x3)>u​(x4)u(x_{3})>u(x_{4}). Sinceuuis monotone,
this forces it to be non-increasing. Letx′=min⁡{x1,x3}x^{\prime}=\min\{x_{1},x_{3}\}andx′′=max⁡{x2,x4}x^{\prime\prime}=\max\{x_{2},x_{4}\}.
Then for any pointsy1≤x′y_{1}\leq x^{\prime}andy2≥x′′y_{2}\geq x^{\prime\prime}we haveg​(y1)≤g​(x′)≤g​(x1)<g​(x2)≤g​(x′′)≤g​(y2),\displaystyle g(y_{1})\leq g(x^{\prime})\leq g(x_{1})<g(x_{2})\leq g(x^{\prime\prime})\leq g(y_{2}),u​(y1)≥u​(x′)≥u​(x1)>u​(x2)≥u​(x′′)≥u​(y2),\displaystyle u(y_{1})\geq u(x^{\prime})\geq u(x_{1})>u(x_{2})\geq u(x^{\prime\prime})\geq u(y_{2}),

from which(u​(y2)−u​(y1))​(g​(y2)−g​(y1))<0\bigl(u(y_{2})-u(y_{1})\bigr)\bigl(g(y_{2})-g(y_{1})\bigr)<0.
In the casep>1p>1,
this fact contradicts the equality condition (2.4a).

We now treat separately the casep=1p=1. BySubsection 2.2, we havem≔inf(a,b)g≤u≤sup(a,b)g≕M.m\coloneqq\inf_{(a,b)}g\leq u\leq\sup_{(a,b)}g\eqqcolon M.

In the limity1→a+y_{1}\to a^{+}andy2→b−y_{2}\to b^{-}we haveg​(y1)<u​(y1)g(y_{1})<u(y_{1})andu​(y2)<g​(y2)u(y_{2})<g(y_{2})becauselimy1→a+(u​(y1)−g​(y1))=limy1→a+[(u​(y2)−g​(y1))+(u​(y1)−u​(y2))]≥(u​(x1)−u​(x2))+limy1→a+(u​(y2)−g​(y1))≥(u​(x1)−u​(x2))+(u​(y2)−m)≥u​(x1)−u​(x2)>0\begin{split}\lim_{y_{1}\to a^{+}}\bigl(u(y_{1})-g(y_{1})\bigr)&=\lim_{y_{1}\to a^{+}}\bigl[\bigl(u(y_{2})-g(y_{1})\bigr)+\bigl(u(y_{1})-u(y_{2})\bigr)\bigr]\\
&\geq\bigl(u(x_{1})-u(x_{2})\bigr)+\lim_{y_{1}\to a^{+}}\bigl(u(y_{2})-g(y_{1})\bigr)\\
&\geq\bigl(u(x_{1})-u(x_{2})\bigr)+\bigl(u(y_{2})-m\bigr)\geq u(x_{1})-u(x_{2})>0\end{split}

and similarlylimy2→b−(g​(y2)−u​(y2))≥u​(x1)−u​(x2)>0.\lim_{y_{2}\to b^{-}}\bigl(g(y_{2})-u(y_{2})\bigr)\geq u(x_{1})-u(x_{2})>0.

Therefore there is a non-negligible set of pointsy1,y2y_{1},y_{2}which violate the inequality
(2.4b).
∎

The next lemma contains the explicit calculation of an integral which will be useful to estimate the
fidelity term from below inSubsection 2.2.

## Lemma 2.18.

Leta∈ℝa\in\mathbb{R}andh,C,α∈ℝ+h,C,\alpha\in\mathbb{R}_{+}. Then∫aa+(h/C)1/α(h−C​(x−a)α)p​d​x=K​(p,α)​C−1/α​hp+1/α,\int_{a}^{a+(h/C)^{1/\alpha}}\bigl(h-C(x-a)^{\alpha}\bigr)^{p}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x=K(p,\alpha)C^{-1/\alpha}h^{p+1/\alpha},

whereK​(p,α)=B​(p+1,1/α)α=Γ​(p+1)​Γ​(1/α)/αΓ​(p+1/α+1)=Γ​(p+1)​Γ​(1/α+1)Γ​(p+1/α+1)=(p+1/αp)−1K(p,\alpha)=\frac{\mathrm{B}(p+1,1/\alpha)}{\alpha}=\frac{\Gamma(p+1)\Gamma(1/\alpha)/\alpha}{\Gamma(p+1/\alpha+1)}=\frac{\Gamma(p+1)\Gamma(1/\alpha+1)}{\Gamma(p+1/\alpha+1)}=\binom{p+1/\alpha}{p}^{-1}

andB​(x,y)=∫01tx−1​(1−t)1−y​d​t=Γ​(x)​Γ​(y)/Γ​(x+y)\mathrm{B}(x,y)=\int_{0}^{1}t^{x-1}(1-t)^{1-y}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t=\Gamma(x)\Gamma(y)/\Gamma(x+y)(2.6)

is the Beta function.

## Proof.

After a translation byaaand with the substitutiony=Ch​xαy=\frac{C}{h}x^{\alpha},d​x=(h/C)1/αα​y1/α−1​d​y\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x=\frac{(h/C)^{1/\alpha}}{\alpha}y^{1/\alpha-1}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}y, we have∫aa+(h/C)1/α(h−C​(x−a)α)p​d​x=∫0(h/C)1/α(h−C​xα)p​d​x=hp​∫0(h/C)1/α(1−Ch​xα)p​d​x=hp​(h/C)1/αα​∫01(1−y)p​y1/α−1​d​y=hp​(h/C)1/αα​B​(p+1,1/α).∎\begin{split}\int_{a}^{a+(h/C)^{1/\alpha}}\bigl(h-C(x-a)^{\alpha}\bigr)^{p}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x&=\int_{0}^{(h/C)^{1/\alpha}}(h-Cx^{\alpha})^{p}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x=h^{p}\int_{0}^{(h/C)^{1/\alpha}}\left(1-\frac{C}{h}x^{\alpha}\right)^{p}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\\
&=\frac{h^{p}(h/C)^{1/\alpha}}{\alpha}\int_{0}^{1}(1-y)^{p}y^{1/\alpha-1}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}y\\
&=\frac{h^{p}(h/C)^{1/\alpha}}{\alpha}\mathrm{B}(p+1,1/\alpha).\qed\end{split}

Ifggsatisfies alsog​(x)−g−​(b)≥−C​(b−x)αg(x)-g^{-}(b)\geq-C(b-x)^{\alpha}for everyx∈(a,b)x\in(a,b)andk≔g−​(b)−sup(a,b)u≥0,k\coloneqq g^{-}(b)-\sup_{(a,b)}u\geq 0,

then∥u−g∥Lp​((a,b))p≥K​(p,α)​C−1/α​(hp+1/α+kp+1/α).\lVert u-g\rVert_{L^{p}\bigl((a,b)\bigr)}^{p}\geq K(p,\alpha)C^{-1/\alpha}\bigl(h^{p+1/\alpha}+k^{p+1/\alpha}\bigr).(2.7)

## Proof.

In the interval(a,a+(h/C)1/α)\bigl(a,a+(h/C)^{1/\alpha}\bigr)we haveg​(x)≤g+​(a)+C​(x−a)α≤g+​(a)+h≤u​(x),g(x)\leq g^{+}(a)+C(x-a)^{\alpha}\leq g^{+}(a)+h\leq u(x),

therefore, bySubsection 2.2,∥u−g∥Lp​((a,b))p≥∫aa+(h/C)1/α(u​(x)−g​(x))p​d​x≥∫aa+(h/C)1/α(h−C​(x−a)α)p​d​x≥K​(p,α)​C−1/α​hp+1/α.\begin{split}\lVert u-g\rVert_{L^{p}((a,b))}^{p}&\geq\int_{a}^{a+(h/C)^{1/\alpha}}\bigl(u(x)-g(x)\bigr)^{p}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\geq\int_{a}^{a+(h/C)^{1/\alpha}}\bigl(h-C(x-a)^{\alpha}\bigr)^{p}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\\
&\geq K(p,\alpha)C^{-1/\alpha}h^{p+1/\alpha}.\end{split}

therefore∥u−g∥Lp​((a,b))p≥∫aa+(h/C)1/α(u​(x)−g​(x))p​d​x+∫b−(k/C)1/αb(g​(x)−u​(x))p​d​x≥∫aa+(h/C)1/α(h−C​(x−a)α)p​d​x+∫b−(k/C)1/αb(k−C​(b−x)α)p​d​x≥K​(p,α)​C−1/α​(hp+1/α+kp+1/α).∎\begin{split}\lVert u-g\rVert_{L^{p}((a,b))}^{p}&\geq\int_{a}^{a+(h/C)^{1/\alpha}}\bigl(u(x)-g(x)\bigr)^{p}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x+\int_{b-(k/C)^{1/\alpha}}^{b}\bigl(g(x)-u(x)\bigr)^{p}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\\
&\geq\int_{a}^{a+(h/C)^{1/\alpha}}\bigl(h-C(x-a)^{\alpha}\bigr)^{p}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x+\int_{b-(k/C)^{1/\alpha}}^{b}\bigl(k-C(b-x)^{\alpha}\bigr)^{p}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\\
&\geq K(p,\alpha)C^{-1/\alpha}\bigl(h^{p+1/\alpha}+k^{p+1/\alpha}\bigr).\qed\end{split}g+​(a)g^{+}(a)aabbg+​(a)+C​(x−a)αg^{+}(a)+C(x-a)^{\alpha}g−​(b)−C​(b−x)αg^{-}(b)-C(b-x)^{\alpha}inf(a,b)u=g+​(a)+h\displaystyle\inf_{(a,b)}u=g^{+}(a)+hsup(a,b)u=g−​(b)−k\displaystyle\sup_{(a,b)}u=g^{-}(b)-kg−​(b)g^{-}(b)g​(x)g(x)u​(x)u(x)Figure 1:depiction of the situation described inSubsection 2.2.

## Definition 2.20.

Givenn∈ℕ+n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}, an exponentp>0p>0and non-negative real numbersx1,…,xn∈[0,∞)x_{1},\dots,x_{n}\in[0,\infty), theirpp-mean isMp​(x1,…,xn)=(1n​∑i=1nxip)1/p.M_{p}(x_{1},\dots,x_{n})=\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}x_{i}^{p}\right)^{1/p}.

If is well known thatMp≤MqM_{p}\leq M_{q}forp<qp<q.
Moreover, clearlyMp​(x1,…,xn)=0M_{p}(x_{1},\dots,x_{n})=0if and only ifx1=⋯=xn=0x_{1}=\dots=x_{n}=0.

## 2.3Perspective of a convex function

Letφ:[0,∞)→ℝ\varphi:[0,\infty)\to\mathbb{R}be a convex function which is continuous in0.222One may extend it toφ:ℝ→ℝ∪{∞}\varphi:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}\cup\{\infty\}by settingφ|(−∞,0)=∞\varphi\rvert_{(-\infty,0)}=\infty,
but of course this extension is no longer continuous in0.

Theperspectiveofφ\varphiisφ:[0,∞)×(0,∞)→ℝ\varphi:[0,\infty)\times(0,\infty)\to\mathbb{R}given byφ​(t,s)=s​φ​(t/s)\varphi(t,s)=s\varphi(t/s).
It is clearly continuous, and it is also convex (see[5, Section 3.2.6]).

It is well known that the difference quotient functiont↦φ​(t)−φ​(0)tt\mapsto\frac{\varphi(t)-\varphi(0)}{t}is non-decreasing.
As a consequence, we can define theasymptotic slopeφ′​(∞)=limt→∞φ​(t)t=limt→∞φ​(t)−φ​(0)t=supt>0φ​(t)−φ​(0)t∈ℝ∪{∞}\varphi^{\prime}(\infty)=\lim_{t\to\infty}\frac{\varphi(t)}{t}=\lim_{t\to\infty}\frac{\varphi(t)-\varphi(0)}{t}=\sup_{t>0}\frac{\varphi(t)-\varphi(0)}{t}\in\mathbb{R}\cup\{\infty\}

and therecession functionφ∞:[0,∞)→(−∞,∞]\varphi^{\infty}:[0,\infty)\to(-\infty,\infty]φ∞​(t)=limr→∞φ​(r​t)r=limr→∞φ​(r​t)−φ​(0)r=supr>0φ​(r​t)−φ​(0)r∈ℝ∪{∞}.\varphi^{\infty}(t)=\lim_{r\to\infty}\frac{\varphi(rt)}{r}=\lim_{r\to\infty}\frac{\varphi(rt)-\varphi(0)}{r}=\sup_{r>0}\frac{\varphi(rt)-\varphi(0)}{r}\in\mathbb{R}\cup\{\infty\}.

It is immediate to verify thatφ∞​(t)=φ′​(∞)​t\varphi^{\infty}(t)=\varphi^{\prime}(\infty)tfor everyt∈[0,∞)t\in[0,\infty),
with the usual convention∞⋅0=0\infty\cdot 0=0.
Indeed fort=0t=0we haveφ∞​(0)=0=φ′​(∞)​0\varphi^{\infty}(0)=0=\varphi^{\prime}(\infty)0, and fort>0t>0we haveφ∞​(t)=limr→∞φ​(r​t)r=t​limr→∞φ​(r​t)r​t=t​limr→∞φ​(r)r=t​φ′​(∞).\varphi^{\infty}(t)=\lim_{r\to\infty}\frac{\varphi(rt)}{r}=t\lim_{r\to\infty}\frac{\varphi(rt)}{rt}=t\lim_{r\to\infty}\frac{\varphi(r)}{r}=t\varphi^{\prime}(\infty).

Moreover, ifφ​(0)=0\varphi(0)=0, thenφ​(t,s)\varphi(t,s)is non-increasing inss: indeed,1t​φ​(t,s)=st​φ​(t/s)=φ​(t/s)−φ​(0)t/s\frac{1}{t}\varphi(t,s)=\frac{s}{t}\varphi(t/s)=\frac{\varphi(t/s)-\varphi(0)}{t/s}

is non-decreasing int/st/s, therefore non-increasing inss.

## Lemma 2.21.

Using the notationφs=φ​(⋅,s)\varphi_{s}=\varphi(\,\cdot\,,s)to denote the perspective, we have the following
rules of commutation with the Legendre transform: for everys>0s>0(φs)∗\displaystyle(\varphi_{s})^{*}=s​φ∗,\displaystyle=s\varphi^{*},(φ∗)s\displaystyle(\varphi^{*})_{s}=(s​φ)∗.\displaystyle=(s\varphi)^{*}.

## Proof.

Straightforward computations show that, for everyτ∈ℝ\tau\in\mathbb{R},(φs)∗​(τ)=supt≥0(τ​t−φs​(t))=supt≥0(τ​t−s​φ​(t/s))=s⋅supt≥0(τ​t/s−φ​(t/s))=s⋅supu≥0(τ​u−φ​(u))=s​φ∗​(τ)\begin{split}(\varphi_{s})^{*}(\tau)&=\sup_{t\geq 0}\bigl(\tau t-\varphi_{s}(t)\bigr)=\sup_{t\geq 0}\bigl(\tau t-s\varphi(t/s)\bigr)\\
&=s\cdot\sup_{t\geq 0}\bigl(\tau t/s-\varphi(t/s)\bigr)=s\cdot\sup_{u\geq 0}\bigl(\tau u-\varphi(u)\bigr)=s\varphi^{*}(\tau)\end{split}

and(φ∗)s​(τ)=s​φ∗​(τ/s)=s⋅supt≥0((τ/s)​t−φ​(t))=supt≥0(τ​t−s​φ​(t))=(s​φ)∗​(τ).∎(\varphi^{*})_{s}(\tau)=s\varphi^{*}(\tau/s)=s\cdot\sup_{t\geq 0}\bigl((\tau/s)t-\varphi(t)\bigr)=\sup_{t\geq 0}\bigl(\tau t-s\varphi(t)\bigr)=(s\varphi)^{*}(\tau).\qed

## Proposition 2.22.

Ifφ′​(∞)∈ℝ\varphi^{\prime}(\infty)\in\mathbb{R}is finite, then the perspectiveφ:[0,∞)×(0,∞)→ℝ\varphi:[0,\infty)\times(0,\infty)\to\mathbb{R}can be extended continuously toφ:[0,∞)×[0,∞)→ℝ\varphi:[0,\infty)\times[0,\infty)\to\mathbb{R}settingφ​(t,0)=φ∞​(t)=φ′​(∞)​t\varphi(t,0)=\varphi^{\infty}(t)=\varphi^{\prime}(\infty)tand this extension is convex.

## “Specific” proof.333This proof uses more specific properties of the perspective.

Since for everyt∈[0,∞)t\in[0,\infty)we have the directional limitlims→0+φ​(t,s)=lims→0+s​φ​(t/s)=limr→∞φ​(r​t)/r=φ∞​(t),\lim_{s\to 0^{+}}\varphi(t,s)=\lim_{s\to 0^{+}}s\varphi(t/s)=\lim_{r\to\infty}\varphi(rt)/r=\varphi^{\infty}(t),

settingφ​(t,0)=φ∞​(t)=φ′​(∞)​t\varphi(t,0)=\varphi^{\infty}(t)=\varphi^{\prime}(\infty)tis the only possible choice that may render the
extension continuous. Let us verify that it is indeed the case.

Fromφ​(t)−φ​(0)t≤φ′​(∞)\frac{\varphi(t)-\varphi(0)}{t}\leq\varphi^{\prime}(\infty)we get the upper boundφ​(t)≤φ′​(∞)​t+φ​(0)\varphi(t)\leq\varphi^{\prime}(\infty)t+\varphi(0), valid for everyt∈[0,∞)t\in[0,\infty).

We claim that the Legendre transformφ∗​(σ)∈ℝ\varphi^{*}(\sigma)\in\mathbb{R}is finite for everyσ∈(−∞,φ′​(∞))\sigma\in\bigl(-\infty,\varphi^{\prime}(\infty)\bigr).444Notice that in general it may beφ∗​(φ′​(∞))=∞\varphi^{*}\bigl(\varphi^{\prime}(\infty)\bigr)=\infty. This happens whenφ′​(∞)​t−φ​(t)\varphi^{\prime}(\infty)t-\varphi(t)does not go to zero ast→∞t\to\infty, for instanceφ​(t)=o​(t)\varphi(t)=o(t)butφ​(t)↛0\varphi(t)\not\to 0. This implies thatφ∗​(σ)=supt∈[0,∞)(σ​t−φ​(t))=max⁡{supt∈[0,T](σ​t−φ​(t)),supt∈[T,∞)(σ​t−φ​(t))}=max⁡{maxt∈[0,T]⁡(σ​t−φ​(t)),supt∈[T,∞)(−φ​(0))}<∞\begin{split}\varphi^{*}(\sigma)&=\sup_{t\in[0,\infty)}\bigl(\sigma t-\varphi(t)\bigr)=\max\left\{\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\bigl(\sigma t-\varphi(t)\bigr),\sup_{t\in[T,\infty)}\bigl(\sigma t-\varphi(t)\bigr)\right\}\\
&=\max\left\{\max_{t\in[0,T]}\bigl(\sigma t-\varphi(t)\bigr),\sup_{t\in[T,\infty)}\bigl(-\varphi(0)\bigr)\right\}<\infty\end{split}

is finite. This fact provides us with the lower boundφ​(t)≥σ​t−φ∗​(σ)\varphi(t)\geq\sigma t-\varphi^{*}(\sigma),
valid for everyt∈[0,∞)t\in[0,\infty).

The lower bound and upper bound can be combined, evaluated att/st/sand multiplied byss, yieldingσ​t−φ∗​(σ)​s≤s​φ​(t/s)≤φ′​(∞)​t+φ​(0)​s,∀(t,s)∈[0,∞)×(0,∞).\sigma t-\varphi^{*}(\sigma)s\leq s\varphi(t/s)\leq\varphi^{\prime}(\infty)t+\varphi(0)s,\qquad\forall(t,s)\in[0,\infty)\times(0,\infty).

s\to 0^{+}\end{subarray}}\varphi(t,s)=\limsup_{\begin{subarray}{c}t\to t_{0}\\

andlim inft→t0s→0+φ​(t,s)=lim inft→t0s→0+s​φ​(t/s)≥lim inft→t0s→0+(σ​t−φ∗​(σ)​s)=σ​t0.\liminf_{\begin{subarray}{c}t\to t_{0}\\
s\to 0^{+}\end{subarray}}\varphi(t,s)=\liminf_{\begin{subarray}{c}t\to t_{0}\\

s\to 0^{+}\end{subarray}}\varphi(t,s)=\varphi^{\prime}(\infty)t_{0}=\varphi(t_{0},0),

It is a general fact that a continuous extension is necessarily convex.
∎

## “General” proof.555This proof uses only the existence of the directional limit.

Since for everyt∈[0,∞)t\in[0,\infty)we have the directional limitlims→0+φ​(t,s)=lims→0+s​φ​(t/s)=limr→∞φ​(r​t)/r=φ∞​(t),\lim_{s\to 0^{+}}\varphi(t,s)=\lim_{s\to 0^{+}}s\varphi(t/s)=\lim_{r\to\infty}\varphi(rt)/r=\varphi^{\infty}(t),

settingφ​(t,0)=φ∞​(t)=φ′​(∞)​t\varphi(t,0)=\varphi^{\infty}(t)=\varphi^{\prime}(\infty)tis the only possible choice that may render the
extension continuous.

First of all, observe that the extension is convex. To this end, let(t0,s0),(t1,s1)∈[0,∞)×[0,∞)(t_{0},s_{0}),(t_{1},s_{1})\in[0,\infty)\times[0,\infty),τ∈[0,1]\tau\in[0,1]and(tτ,sτ)=(1−τ)​(t0,s0)+τ​(t1,s1)(t_{\tau},s_{\tau})=(1-\tau)(t_{0},s_{0})+\tau(t_{1},s_{1}). For everyε>0\varepsilon>0we have(t0,s0+ε),(t1,s1+ε)∈[0,∞)×(0,∞)(t_{0},s_{0}+\varepsilon),(t_{1},s_{1}+\varepsilon)\in[0,\infty)\times(0,\infty)and(tτ,sτ+ε)=(1−τ)​(t0,s0+ε)+τ​(t1,s1+ε)(t_{\tau},s_{\tau}+\varepsilon)=(1-\tau)(t_{0},s_{0}+\varepsilon)+\tau(t_{1},s_{1}+\varepsilon), thereforeφ​(tτ,sτ+ε)≤(1−τ)​φ​(t0,s0+ε)+τ​φ​(t1,s1+ε).\varphi(t_{\tau},s_{\tau}+\varepsilon)\leq(1-\tau)\varphi(t_{0},s_{0}+\varepsilon)+\tau\varphi(t_{1},s_{1}+\varepsilon).

Sendingε→0+\varepsilon\to 0^{+}and using the directional continuity inssyieldsφ​(tτ,sτ)≤(1−τ)​φ​(t0,s0)+τ​φ​(t1,s1),\varphi(t_{\tau},s_{\tau})\leq(1-\tau)\varphi(t_{0},s_{0})+\tau\varphi(t_{1},s_{1}),

hence the extension is convex.

Let us verify that it is continuous.
Notice that the extensionφ​(t,0)=φ′​(∞)​t\varphi(t,0)=\varphi^{\prime}(\infty)tis a continuous function oftt.
Fixt0∈(0,∞)t_{0}\in(0,\infty)andε>0\varepsilon>0. There existsδ>0\delta>0such thatt0−δ≥0t_{0}-\delta\geq 0and|φ​(t0±δ,0)−φ​(t0,0)|≤ε/2.\lvert\varphi(t_{0}\pm\delta,0)-\varphi(t_{0},0)\rvert\leq\varepsilon/2.

By the continuity inss, there existsη>0\eta>0such that for everys∈[0,η]s\in[0,\eta]we have|φ​(t0,s)−φ​(t0,0)|\displaystyle\lvert\varphi(t_{0},s)-\varphi(t_{0},0)\rvert≤ε/2,\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon/2,|φ​(t0±δ,s)−φ​(t0±δ,0)|\displaystyle\lvert\varphi(t_{0}\pm\delta,s)-\varphi(t_{0}\pm\delta,0)\rvert≤ε/2.\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon/2.

By the triangle inequality we deduce also|φ​(t0±δ,s)−φ​(t0,0)|≤ε\lvert\varphi(t_{0}\pm\delta,s)-\varphi(t_{0},0)\rvert\leq\varepsilon.
Let(t,s)∈[t0−δ,t0+δ]×[0,η](t,s)\in[t_{0}-\delta,t_{0}+\delta]\times[0,\eta]be an arbitrary point in this neighborhood of(t0,0)(t_{0},0).
By convexity we have the upper boundφ​(t,s)≤max⁡{φ​(t0±δ,0),φ​(t0±δ,η)}≤φ​(t0,0)+ε.\varphi(t,s)\leq\max\{\varphi(t_{0}\pm\delta,0),\varphi(t_{0}\pm\delta,\eta)\}\leq\varphi(t_{0},0)+\varepsilon.

To find a lower bound, assume thatt0≤t≤t0+δt_{0}\leq t\leq t_{0}+\delta;
the symmetric caset0−δ≤t≤t0t_{0}-\delta\leq t\leq t_{0}is analogous.
We can writet0=(1−τ)​(t0−δ)+τ​tt_{0}=(1-\tau)(t_{0}-\delta)+\tau twithτ=δ/(t−t0+δ)∈[1/2,1]\tau=\delta/(t-t_{0}+\delta)\in[1/2,1], thereforeφ​(t0,s)≤(1−τ)​φ​(t0−δ,s)+τ​φ​(t,s),\varphi(t_{0},s)\leq(1-\tau)\varphi(t_{0}-\delta,s)+\tau\varphi(t,s),

from whichφ​(t,s)≥1τ​φ​(t0,s)−1−ττ​φ​(t0−δ,s)≥1τ​(φ​(t0,0)−ε)−1−ττ​(φ​(t0,0)+ε)=φ​(t0,0)−(1−2τ)​ε≥φ​(t0,0)−3​ε.\begin{split}\varphi(t,s)&\geq\frac{1}{\tau}\varphi(t_{0},s)-\frac{1-\tau}{\tau}\varphi(t_{0}-\delta,s)\geq\frac{1}{\tau}\bigl(\varphi(t_{0},0)-\varepsilon\bigr)-\frac{1-\tau}{\tau}\bigl(\varphi(t_{0},0)+\varepsilon\bigr)\\
&=\varphi(t_{0},0)-\left(1-\frac{2}{\tau}\right)\varepsilon\geq\varphi(t_{0},0)-3\varepsilon.\end{split}

Combined with the upper bound, this shows that|φ​(t,s)−φ​(t0,0)|≤3​ε\lvert\varphi(t,s)-\varphi(t_{0},0)\rvert\leq 3\varepsilonfor every(t,s)∈[t0−δ,t0+δ]×[0,η](t,s)\in[t_{0}-\delta,t_{0}+\delta]\times[0,\eta], proving the continuity in(t0,0)(t_{0},0), fort0∈(0,∞)t_{0}\in(0,\infty).

The continuity in(0,0)(0,0)is proved similarly. Givenε>0\varepsilon>0there existsδ>0\delta>0such that|φ​(δ,0)−φ​(0,0)|\displaystyle\lvert\varphi(\delta,0)-\varphi(0,0)\rvert≤ε/2,\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon/2,|φ​(2​δ,0)−φ​(0,0)|\displaystyle\lvert\varphi(2\delta,0)-\varphi(0,0)\rvert≤ε/2.\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon/2.

By the continuity inss, there existsη>0\eta>0such that for everys∈[0,η]s\in[0,\eta]we have|φ​(0,s)−φ​(0,0)|\displaystyle\lvert\varphi(0,s)-\varphi(0,0)\rvert≤ε/2,\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon/2,|φ​(δ,s)−φ​(δ,0)|\displaystyle\lvert\varphi(\delta,s)-\varphi(\delta,0)\rvert≤ε/2,\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon/2,|φ​(2​δ,s)−φ​(2​δ,0)|\displaystyle\lvert\varphi(2\delta,s)-\varphi(2\delta,0)\rvert≤ε/2.\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon/2.

By the triangle inequality we infer|φ​(i​δ,s)−φ​(0,0)|≤ε\lvert\varphi(i\delta,s)-\varphi(0,0)\rvert\leq\varepsilonfori=0,1,2i=0,1,2.
If(t,s)∈[0,2​δ]×[0,η](t,s)\in[0,2\delta]\times[0,\eta]is an arbitrary point in this neighborhood of the origin,
with a similar argument as before we get|φ​(t,s)−φ​(0,0)|≤3​ε\lvert\varphi(t,s)-\varphi(0,0)\rvert\leq 3\varepsilon, hence proving the
continuity.
∎

## 3LinearTV\operatorname{TV}

In this section we study the minimization of the functionalF​(u)=TV⁡(u)+λ​∥u−g∥pγF(u)=\operatorname{TV}(u)+\lambda\lVert u-g\rVert_{p}^{\gamma}

with linear total variation and fidelity with respect to an input datumgg.
We classify situations where the minimizeruuis not the inputggitself (Subsection 3.1)
and situations where the minimizeruuis indeed the inputgg(Subsection 3.2).

Our results are based on some conditions ongg(α\alpha-growth inSubsection 2.1,
monotonicity, piecewise monotonicity) and on specific ranges of the parametersp,γ,λp,\gamma,\lambda.
The complementarity of these ranges provides a full characterization and demonstrates the sharpness
of our results (seeSubsection 3.2).

## 3.1The input is not a minimizer

We start with a model situation where the inputggis taken to be the Cantor–Lebesgue–Vitali functionχ∈C​([0,1];[0,1])\chi\in C([0,1];[0,1]).
Subsequently, this example will be generalized inTheorem 3.4to functions
with at leastα\alpha-growth at one endpoint (seeSubsection 2.1).

## Theorem 3.1.

Letχ∈C​([0,1];[0,1])\chi\in C([0,1];[0,1])be the Cantor–Lebesgue–Vitali function andλ,γ>0\lambda,\gamma>0,p∈[1,∞]p\in[1,\infty].
Ifγ>(1p⋅log⁡3log⁡2+1)−1\gamma>\left(\frac{1}{p}\cdot\frac{\log 3}{\log 2}+1\right)^{-1}

then the functionχ\chiis not a minimizer of the functionalF​(u)=TV⁡(u)+λ​∥u−χ∥pγ.F(u)=\operatorname{TV}(u)+\lambda\lVert u-\chi\rVert_{p}^{\gamma}.

## Proof.

Givenn∈ℕ+n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}letχn=2−n∨χ∧(1−2−n)∈C​([0,1];[2−n,1−2−n])\chi_{n}=2^{-n}\vee\chi\wedge(1-2^{-n})\in C([0,1];[2^{-n},1-2^{-n}]). We haveF​(χ)=TV⁡(χ)=1F(\chi)=\operatorname{TV}(\chi)=1andTV⁡(χn)=1−2−n+1\operatorname{TV}(\chi_{n})=1-2^{-n+1}. Moreover, forp=∞p=\inftywe have∥χn−χ∥p=2−n\lVert\chi_{n}-\chi\rVert_{p}=2^{-n}, whereas forp∈[1,∞)p\in[1,\infty)we have∥χn−χ∥pp=2​∫03−nχ​(x)p​d​x≤2​∫03−nχ​(x)p−1​χ​(x)​d​x≤2​∥χ∥L∞​([0,3−n])p−1​∫03−nχ​(x)​d​x=2⋅2−n​(p−1)​12​2−n​3−n=2−n​p​3−n,\begin{split}\lVert\chi_{n}-\chi\rVert_{p}^{p}&=2\int_{0}^{3^{-n}}\chi(x)^{p}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\leq 2\int_{0}^{3^{-n}}\chi(x)^{p-1}\chi(x)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\leq 2\lVert\chi\rVert_{L^{\infty}([0,3^{-n}])}^{p-1}\int_{0}^{3^{-n}}\chi(x)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\\
&=2\cdot 2^{-n(p-1)}\frac{1}{2}2^{-n}3^{-n}=2^{-np}3^{-n},\end{split}

hence∥χn−χ∥p≤2−n​3−n/p\lVert\chi_{n}-\chi\rVert_{p}\leq 2^{-n}3^{-n/p}in both cases (interpreting−n/p=0-n/p=0forp=∞p=\infty). From this we get thatF​(χn)≤1−2−n+1+λ​2−n​γ​3−n​γ/p,F(\chi_{n})\leq 1-2^{-n+1}+\lambda 2^{-n\gamma}3^{-n\gamma/p},

thereforeF​(χn)<F​(χ)⇔λ​2−n​γ​3−n​γ/p<2−n+1F(\chi_{n})<F(\chi)\Leftrightarrow\lambda 2^{-n\gamma}3^{-n\gamma/p}<2^{-n+1}, i.e. if and only ifλ2<2(γ−1)​n3γp​n=exp(((γ−1)log2+γplog3)n).\frac{\lambda}{2}<2^{(\gamma-1)n}3^{\frac{\gamma}{p}n}=\exp\mathopen{}\left(\left((\gamma-1)\log 2+\frac{\gamma}{p}\log 3\right)n\right).

Fornnlarge enough this is satisfied if(γ−1)​log⁡2+γp​log⁡3>0⟺(1−γ)​log⁡2<γp​log⁡3⟺1−γγ<1p⋅log⁡3log⁡2⟺1γ<1p⋅log⁡3log⁡2+1.∎(\gamma-1)\log 2+\frac{\gamma}{p}\log 3>0\Longleftrightarrow(1-\gamma)\log 2<\frac{\gamma}{p}\log 3\Longleftrightarrow\frac{1-\gamma}{\gamma}<\frac{1}{p}\cdot\frac{\log 3}{\log 2}\Longleftrightarrow\frac{1}{\gamma}<\frac{1}{p}\cdot\frac{\log 3}{\log 2}+1.\qed

## Remark 3.2.

The previous statement holds true for the functionalF​(u)=TVφ,θ⁡(u)+λ​∥u−χ∥pγF(u)=\operatorname{TV}\!\!_{\varphi,\theta}(u)+\lambda\lVert u-\chi\rVert_{p}^{\gamma}

whereTVφ,θ⁡(u)=∫01φ​(|u′|)+|Dc​u|​((0,1))+∑Juθ​(|u+−u−|)\operatorname{TV}\!\!_{\varphi,\theta}(u)=\int_{0}^{1}\varphi(\lvert u^{\prime}\rvert)+\lvert D^{c}u\rvert((0,1))+\sum_{J_{u}}\theta(\lvert u^{+}-u^{-}\rvert)

is the non-linear total variation.666The conditionlimt→∞φ​(t)/t=limt→0+θ​(t)/t=1\lim_{t\to\infty}\varphi(t)/t=\lim_{t\to 0^{+}}\theta(t)/t=1is not required.This is becauseTVφ,θ\operatorname{TV}_{\varphi,\theta}coincides withTV\operatorname{TV}for bothχ\chiand the competitorχn\chi_{n}.

The next lemma is used in the proof ofTheorem 3.4. We will need it only withℓ=sup(a,c]g\ell=\sup_{(a,c]}g, but we prefer to give a more general statement, which might be of broader interest.

## Lemma 3.3.

Letg∈BV⁡((a,b))g\in\operatorname{BV}\bigl((a,b)\bigr),c∈(a,b)c\in(a,b)andinf(a,c]g≤ℓ≤sup(a,c]g.\inf_{(a,c]}g\leq\ell\leq\sup_{(a,c]}g.

Defineu:(a,b)→ℝu:(a,b)\to\mathbb{R}such thatu​(x)={ℓx∈(a,c),g​(x)x∈(c,b),u(x)=\begin{cases}\ell&x\in(a,c),\\
g(x)&x\in(c,b),\end{cases}

anduuis regular incc. Thenu∈BV⁡((a,b))u\in\operatorname{BV}\bigl((a,b)\bigr)andTV⁡(g;(a,b))≥TV⁡(u;(a,b))+|ℓ−g+​(a)|.\operatorname{TV}\bigl(g;(a,b)\bigr)\geq\operatorname{TV}\bigl(u;(a,b)\bigr)+\lvert\ell-g^{+}(a)\rvert.

## Proof.

Sinceuuis constant in(a,c)(a,c), is regular incc, andu=gu=gin(c,b)(c,b), we haveTV⁡(u;(a,b))=TV⁡(u;(a,c))+|u−​(c)−u+​(c)|+TV⁡(u;(c,b))=|ℓ−g+​(c)|+TV⁡(g;(c,b)),\begin{split}\operatorname{TV}\bigl(u;(a,b)\bigr)=\operatorname{TV}\bigl(u;(a,c)\bigr)+\lvert u^{-}(c)-u^{+}(c)\rvert+\operatorname{TV}\bigl(u;(c,b)\bigr)=\lvert\ell-g^{+}(c)\rvert+\operatorname{TV}\bigl(g;(c,b)\bigr),\end{split}

henceu∈BV⁡((a,b))u\in\operatorname{BV}\bigl((a,b)\bigr).
Letm=inf(a,c]gm=\inf_{(a,c]}gandM=sup(a,c]gM=\sup_{(a,c]}g.
For everyε>0\varepsilon>0there existsxε′,xε′′∈(a,c]x^{\prime}_{\varepsilon},x^{\prime\prime}_{\varepsilon}\in(a,c]withxε′≤xε′′x^{\prime}_{\varepsilon}\leq x^{\prime\prime}_{\varepsilon}such that|g​(xε′)−m|≤ε∧|g​(xε′′)−M|≤εor|g​(xε′)−M|≤ε∧|g​(xε′′)−m|≤ε.\displaystyle\lvert g(x^{\prime}_{\varepsilon})-m\rvert\leq\varepsilon\quad\wedge\quad\lvert g(x^{\prime\prime}_{\varepsilon})-M\rvert\leq\varepsilon\qquad\text{or}\qquad\lvert g(x^{\prime}_{\varepsilon})-M\rvert\leq\varepsilon\quad\wedge\quad\lvert g(x^{\prime\prime}_{\varepsilon})-m\rvert\leq\varepsilon.

Let us treat the first alternative; the second is analogous.
Exploiting|m−M|=|m−ℓ|+|ℓ−M|\lvert m-M\rvert=\lvert m-\ell\rvert+\lvert\ell-M\rvert, we can estimateTV⁡(g;(a,b))=TV⁡(g;(a,xε′])+TV⁡(g;[xε′,xε′′])+TV⁡(g;[xε′′,c])+TV⁡(g;[c,b))≥|g+​(a)−g​(xε′)|+|g​(xε′)−g​(xε′′)|+|g​(xε′′)−g​(c)|+|g​(c)−g+​(c)|+TV⁡(g;(c,b))≥|g+​(a)−m|+|m−M|+|M−g​(c)|+|g​(c)−g+​(c)|+TV⁡(g;(c,b))−4​ε=|g+​(a)−m|+|m−ℓ|+|ℓ−M|+|M−g​(c)|+|g​(c)−g+​(c)|+TV⁡(g;(c,b))−4​ε≥|g+​(a)−ℓ|+|ℓ−g+​(c)|+TV⁡(g;(c,b))−4​ε=|g+​(a)−ℓ|+TV⁡(u;(a,b))−4​ε.\begin{split}&\operatorname{TV}\bigl(g;(a,b)\bigr)\\
&=\operatorname{TV}\bigl(g;(a,x^{\prime}_{\varepsilon}]\bigr)+\operatorname{TV}\bigl(g;[x^{\prime}_{\varepsilon},x^{\prime\prime}_{\varepsilon}]\bigr)+\operatorname{TV}\bigl(g;[x^{\prime\prime}_{\varepsilon},c]\bigr)+\operatorname{TV}\bigl(g;[c,b)\bigr)\\
&\geq\lvert g^{+}(a)-g(x^{\prime}_{\varepsilon})\rvert+\lvert g(x^{\prime}_{\varepsilon})-g(x^{\prime\prime}_{\varepsilon})\rvert+\lvert g(x^{\prime\prime}_{\varepsilon})-g(c)\rvert+\lvert g(c)-g^{+}(c)\rvert+\operatorname{TV}\bigl(g;(c,b)\bigr)\\
&\geq\lvert g^{+}(a)-m\rvert+\lvert m-M\rvert+\lvert M-g(c)\rvert+\lvert g(c)-g^{+}(c)\rvert+\operatorname{TV}\bigl(g;(c,b)\bigr)-4\varepsilon\\
&=\lvert g^{+}(a)-m\rvert+\lvert m-\ell\rvert+\lvert\ell-M\rvert+\lvert M-g(c)\rvert+\lvert g(c)-g^{+}(c)\rvert+\operatorname{TV}\bigl(g;(c,b)\bigr)-4\varepsilon\\
&\geq\lvert g^{+}(a)-\ell\rvert+\lvert\ell-g^{+}(c)\rvert+\operatorname{TV}\bigl(g;(c,b)\bigr)-4\varepsilon\\
&=\lvert g^{+}(a)-\ell\rvert+\operatorname{TV}\bigl(u;(a,b)\bigr)-4\varepsilon.\end{split}

The thesis follows lettingε→0+\varepsilon\to 0^{+}.
∎

## Theorem 3.4.

Letg∈BV⁡((a,b))g\in\operatorname{BV}\bigl((a,b)\bigr)be a function with the property that there existα,c>0\alpha,c>0and0<δ<b−a0<\delta<b-asuch thatg​(x)−g+​(a)≥c​(x−a)αg(x)-g^{+}(a)\geq c(x-a)^{\alpha}for allx∈(a,a+δ]x\in(a,a+\delta].
Ifγ>(1+1p​α)−1=p​αp​α+1\gamma>\left(1+\frac{1}{p\alpha}\right)^{-1}=\frac{p\alpha}{p\alpha+1}(3.1)

thenggis not a minimizer of the functionalF​(u)=TV⁡(u)+λ​∥u−g∥pγF(u)=\operatorname{TV}(u)+\lambda\lVert u-g\rVert_{p}^{\gamma}

for everyp∈[1,∞]p\in[1,\infty]andλ>0\lambda>0.

If instead equality holds in (3.1), the same conclusion is true provided thatλ<K​(p,α)−γ/p​cγ/(p​α)=K​(p,α)−α/(1+p​α)​c1/(1+p​α).\lambda<K(p,\alpha)^{-\gamma/p}c^{\gamma/(p\alpha)}=K(p,\alpha)^{-\alpha/(1+p\alpha)}c^{1/(1+p\alpha)}.(3.2)

## Proof.

Define the non-decreasing functionω:(a,a+δ]→ℝ\omega:(a,a+\delta]\to\mathbb{R}ω​(x)=sup(a,x]g\omega(x)=\sup_{(a,x]}g

and observe that we haveω​(x)≥g​(x)≥g+​(a)+c​(x−a)α\omega(x)\geq g(x)\geq g^{+}(a)+c(x-a)^{\alpha}andω+​(a)=g+​(a)\omega^{+}(a)=g^{+}(a).
Giveny∈(a,a+δ]y\in(a,a+\delta], introduce the auxiliary parameterh=h​(y)=ω​(y)−g+​(a)≥c​(y−a)α>0h=h(y)=\omega(y)-g^{+}(a)\geq c(y-a)^{\alpha}>0and define the truncated functionuy∈BV⁡((a,b))u_{y}\in\operatorname{BV}\bigl((a,b)\bigr)uy​(x)={ω​(y)x∈(a,y],g​(x)x∈(y,b),u_{y}(x)=\begin{cases}\omega(y)&x\in(a,y],\\
g(x)&x\in(y,b),\end{cases}

The following remark highlights a strong structural property of the minimizers ofFFwhen the input

## Remark 3.5.

Assume that the inputggis non-decreasing and letuube a minimizer ofF​(u)=TV⁡(u)+λ​∥u−g∥pγF(u)=\operatorname{TV}(u)+\lambda\lVert u-g\rVert_{p}^{\gamma};uumust be non-decreasing. Definex′\displaystyle x^{\prime}=sup{x∈(a,b):g≤u+​(a)​in​(a,x)},\displaystyle=\sup\{x\in(a,b):g\leq u^{+}(a)\text{ in }(a,x)\},x′′\displaystyle x^{\prime\prime}=inf{x∈(a,b):g≥u−​(b)​in​(x,b)},\displaystyle=\inf\{x\in(a,b):g\geq u^{-}(b)\text{ in }(x,b)\},

and the non-decreasing regular functionu¯​(x)={u+​(a)x∈(a,x′],g​(x)x∈(x′,x′′),u−​(b)x∈[x′′,b).\bar{u}(x)=\begin{cases}u^{+}(a)&x\in(a,x^{\prime}],\\
g(x)&x\in(x^{\prime},x^{\prime\prime}),\\
u^{-}(b)&x\in[x^{\prime\prime},b).\end{cases}

We haveTV⁡(u¯)=u−​(b)−u+​(a)=TV⁡(u)\operatorname{TV}(\bar{u})=u^{-}(b)-u^{+}(a)=\operatorname{TV}(u).
In(a,x′)(a,x^{\prime})we haveg≤u¯≤ug\leq\bar{u}\leq u, whereas in(x′′,b)(x^{\prime\prime},b)the reverse inequalities hold, therefore∥u¯−g∥Lp​((a,x′))\displaystyle\lVert\bar{u}-g\rVert_{L^{p}\bigl((a,x^{\prime})\bigr)}≤∥u−g∥Lp​((a,x′)),\displaystyle\leq\lVert u-g\rVert_{L^{p}\bigl((a,x^{\prime})\bigr)},∥u¯−g∥Lp​((x′′,b))\displaystyle\lVert\bar{u}-g\rVert_{L^{p}\bigl((x^{\prime\prime},b)\bigr)}≤∥u−g∥Lp​((x′′,b)).\displaystyle\leq\lVert u-g\rVert_{L^{p}\bigl((x^{\prime\prime},b)\bigr)}.

In(x′,x′′)(x^{\prime},x^{\prime\prime})we haveu¯=g\bar{u}=g, therefore∥u¯−g∥Lp​((x′,x′′))=0≤∥u−g∥Lp​((x′,x′′)).\lVert\bar{u}-g\rVert_{L^{p}\bigl((x^{\prime},x^{\prime\prime})\bigr)}=0\leq\lVert u-g\rVert_{L^{p}\bigl((x^{\prime},x^{\prime\prime})\bigr)}.

In all three cases, equality holds if and only ifu=u¯u=\bar{u}in the respective intervals.
This shows thatF​(u¯)≤F​(u)F(\bar{u})\leq F(u), with equality if and only ifu=u¯u=\bar{u}in(a,b)(a,b).

This proves that all minimizers must be of the formu¯\bar{u}, although we do not show uniqueness
because the values at the endpointsu+​(a)u^{+}(a)andu−​(b)u^{-}(b)might not be uniquely determined.

The main content ofTheorem 3.4is showing thatu+​(a)>g+​(a)u^{+}(a)>g^{+}(a),
hence a minimizer cannot coincide withgg.

## Theorem 3.6.

Letg∈BV⁡((a,b))g\in\operatorname{BV}\bigl((a,b)\bigr)be a non-decreasing function and letp∈[1,∞]p\in[1,\infty].
If0<λ<(b−a)−1/p0<\lambda<(b-a)^{-1/p}then every minimizeru∈BV⁡((0,1))u\in\operatorname{BV}\bigl((0,1)\bigr)of the functionalF​(u)=TV⁡(u)+λ​∥u−g∥pF(u)=\operatorname{TV}(u)+\lambda\lVert u-g\rVert_{p}

is a constant function. Moreover, the minimizer is unique ifp>1p>1.

## Proof.

We treat the casep<∞p<\infty; the casep=∞p=\inftyfollows from a straightforward adaptation.
Letu∈BV⁡((a,b))u\in\operatorname{BV}\bigl((a,b)\bigr)be a minimizer. BySubsection 2.2uuis non-decreasing.
Moreover, bySubsection 2.2we haveg+​(a)≤u≤g−​(b)g^{+}(a)\leq u\leq g^{-}(b).

Letc∈[u+​(a),u−​(b)]c\in[u^{+}(a),u^{-}(b)]and defineh=c−u+​(a)≥0h=c-u^{+}(a)\geq 0andk=u−​(b)−c≥0k=u^{-}(b)-c\geq 0.
Using the fact that∥u−c∥p≤(b−a)1/p​∥u−c∥∞=(b−a)1/p​max⁡{h,k}≤(b−a)1/p​(h+k)\lVert u-c\rVert_{p}\leq(b-a)^{1/p}\lVert u-c\rVert_{\infty}=(b-a)^{1/p}\max\{h,k\}\leq(b-a)^{1/p}(h+k)

we can estimateF​(u)−F​(c)=TV⁡(u)−TV⁡(c)+λ​(∥u−g∥p−∥c−g∥p)≥u−​(b)−u+​(a)−λ​∥u−c∥p≥h+k−λ​(b−a)1/p​(h+k)=(1−λ​(b−a)1/p)​(h+k).\begin{split}F(u)-F(c)&=\operatorname{TV}(u)-\operatorname{TV}(c)+\lambda(\lVert u-g\rVert_{p}-\lVert c-g\rVert_{p})\geq u^{-}(b)-u^{+}(a)-\lambda\lVert u-c\rVert_{p}\\
&\geq h+k-\lambda(b-a)^{1/p}(h+k)=\bigl(1-\lambda(b-a)^{1/p}\bigr)(h+k).\end{split}

Under the assumption onλ\lambda, the inequalityF​(u)≥F​(c)F(u)\geq F(c)is strict unlessh+k=0h+k=0,
i.e.h=k=0h=k=0, that isu≡cu\equiv c. This proves that every minimizer is constant.

Ifp>1p>1, thenF​(c)=λ​∥c−g∥pF(c)=\lambda\lVert c-g\rVert_{p}is strictly convex, therefore the minimizer is unique.
∎

## 3.2The input is the minimizer

In this section we find conditions that ensure that the inputggis the minimizer.
This has the interesting consequence that,
under the assumptions of eitherTheorem 3.7,Theorem 3.8orTheorem 3.10, ifDc​g≠0D^{c}g\neq 0, then alsoDc​u≠0D^{c}u\neq 0whereuuis the
minimizer ofF​(u)=TV⁡(u)+λ​∥u−g∥pγF(u)=\operatorname{TV}(u)+\lambda\lVert u-g\rVert_{p}^{\gamma},
showing that in general minimizers can have the Cantor part of the derivative.

u(x)&x\in(\delta,1-\delta)\\

has the propertyTV⁡(u~)≤TV⁡(u)\operatorname{TV}(\tilde{u})\leq\operatorname{TV}(u)and∥u~−g∥p<∥u−g∥p\lVert\tilde{u}-g\rVert_{p}<\lVert u-g\rVert_{p}.

We have the estimates∥u−g∥∞≥max⁡{u​(0+),1−u​(1−)}\lVert u-g\rVert_{\infty}\geq\max\{u(0^{+}),1-u(1^{-})\}and∥u−g∥pp≥∫(0,δ)∪(1−δ,1)|u​(x)−g​(x)|p​d​x=∫0δu​(0+)p​d​x+∫1−δ1(1−u​(1−))p​d​x=δ​[u​(0+)p+(1−u​(1−))p].\begin{split}\lVert u-g\rVert_{p}^{p}&\geq\int_{(0,\delta)\cup(1-\delta,1)}\lvert u(x)-g(x)\rvert^{p}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\\

Using these estimates andF​(g)=1F(g)=1we can bound from belowF​(u)=u​(1−)−u​(0+)+λ​∥u−g∥pγ≥1−(h+k)+λ​δγ/p​Mp​(h,k)γ≥F​(g)+(λ​δγ/p−2)​M1​(h,k)F(u)=u(1^{-})-u(0^{+})+\lambda\lVert u-g\rVert_{p}^{\gamma}\geq 1-(h+k)+\lambda\delta^{\gamma/p}M_{p}(h,k)^{\gamma}\geq F(g)+(\lambda\delta^{\gamma/p}-2)M_{1}(h,k)

and this inequality is strict unlessM1​(h,k)=0M_{1}(h,k)=0, i.e.h=k=0h=k=0, that isu​(0+)=0u(0^{+})=0andu​(1−)=1u(1^{-})=1.

In this caseTV⁡(u)=u​(1−)−u​(0+)=1\operatorname{TV}(u)=u(1^{-})-u(0^{+})=1, henceF​(u)=1+λ​∥u−g∥pγ=F​(g)+λ​∥u−g∥pγF(u)=1+\lambda\lVert u-g\rVert_{p}^{\gamma}=F(g)+\lambda\lVert u-g\rVert_{p}^{\gamma}and foruuto be a minimizer we must haveu=gu=g.

## Proposition 3.9.

Letx0<⋯<xnx_{0}<\dots<x_{n}and letg∈BV⁡((x0,xn))g\in\operatorname{BV}\bigl((x_{0},x_{n})\bigr)be a regular function which is monotone

p\left(\frac{\lVert g-\inf_{J}g\rVert_{L^{p-1}(J)}}{\lVert g-\inf_{J}g\rVert_{L^{p}(I)}}\wedge\frac{\lVert g-\sup_{J}g\rVert_{L^{p-1}(J)}}{\lVert g-\sup_{J}g\rVert_{L^{p}(I)}}\right)^{p-1}&\text{otherwise},\end{cases}

then any minimizeru∈BV⁡((x0,xn))u\in\operatorname{BV}\bigl((x_{0},x_{n})\bigr)of the functionalF:BV⁡((x0,xn))→[0,∞)\displaystyle F:\operatorname{BV}\bigl((x_{0},x_{n})\bigr)\to[0,\infty)F​(u)=TV⁡(u)+λ​∥u−g∥pγ.\displaystyle F(u)=\operatorname{TV}(u)+\lambda\lVert u-g\rVert_{p}^{\gamma}.

satisfiesinfIig≤infIiu\displaystyle\inf_{I_{i}}g\leq\inf_{I_{i}}u≤supIiu≤supIig\displaystyle\leq\sup_{I_{i}}u\leq\sup_{I_{i}}g∀i∈{1,…,n}.\displaystyle\forall i\in\{1,\dots,n\}.

## Proof.

LetH=supIg−infIgH=\sup_{I}g-\inf_{I}g.

We may assume thatuuis regular, otherwise regularizing it decreases strictly the total variation without
affecting the fidelity term (seeSubsection 2.1(v)andSubsection 2.1(iv)).
We may also assume thatinfIg≤infIu≤supIu≤supIg\inf_{I}g\leq\inf_{I}u\leq\sup_{I}u\leq\sup_{I}g,
thanks toSubsection 2.1.

Exploiting the symmetry, assume by contradiction that the inequalityinfIig≤infIiu\inf_{I_{i}}g\leq\inf_{I_{i}}uis violated for some indexi∈{1,…,n}i\in\{1,\dots,n\}and thatggis increasing inIiI_{i}.
Lettingm=infIigm=\inf_{I_{i}}g, our goal is to show that the functionu~\tilde{u}such thatu~=u\tilde{u}=uin(x0,xn)∖Ii(x_{0},x_{n})\setminus I_{i}andu~=u∨m\tilde{u}=u\vee minIiI_{i}performs strictly better thanuu,
i.e.F​(u~)<F​(u)F(\tilde{u})<F(u), therefore contradicting the absurd assumption.
To this end we letδ=infIig−supIiu=m−supIiu\delta=\inf_{I_{i}}g-\sup_{I_{i}}u=m-\sup_{I_{i}}u

and we distinguish two cases: eitherδ>0\delta>0, orδ≤0\delta\leq 0.

Caseδ>0\delta>0.We start by showing thatTV⁡(u~;(x0,xn))≤TV⁡(u;(x0,xn))+2​δ\operatorname{TV}\bigl(\tilde{u};(x_{0},x_{n})\bigr)\leq\operatorname{TV}\bigl(u;(x_{0},x_{n})\bigr)+2\delta(3.3)

We splitTV⁡(u;(x0,xn))=TV⁡(u;(x0,xi−1])+TV⁡(u;[xi−1,xi])+TV⁡(u;[xi,xn))\operatorname{TV}\bigl(u;(x_{0},x_{n})\bigr)=\operatorname{TV}\bigl(u;(x_{0},x_{i-1}]\bigr)+\operatorname{TV}\bigl(u;[x_{i-1},x_{i}]\bigr)+\operatorname{TV}\bigl(u;[x_{i},x_{n})\bigr)

and similarly foru~\tilde{u}.

## Theorem 3.10.

Letx0<⋯<xnx_{0}<\dots<x_{n}and letg∈BVreg⁡((x0,xn))g\in\operatorname{BV}_{\operatorname{reg}}\bigl((x_{0},x_{n})\bigr)be a function which is monotone

i.e. there existα,C>0\alpha,C>0such that|g​(x)−g+​(xi−1)|\displaystyle\lvert g(x)-g^{+}(x_{i-1})\rvert≤C​(x−xi−1)α\displaystyle\leq C(x-x_{i-1})^{\alpha}|g​(x)−g−​(xi)|\displaystyle\lvert g(x)-g^{-}(x_{i})\rvert≤C​(xi−x)α\displaystyle\leq C(x_{i}-x)^{\alpha}∀x∈(xi−1,xi).\displaystyle\forall x\in(x_{i-1},x_{i}).

Given some pointsξi∈Ii\xi_{i}\in I_{i}, letIi−=(xi−1,ξi)I_{i}^{-}=(x_{i-1},\xi_{i})andIi+=(ξi,xi)I_{i}^{+}=(\xi_{i},x_{i}).
If the conditions ofSubsection 3.2hold for the intervalsIiI_{i},Ii−I_{i}^{-}andIi+I_{i}^{+},
and in addition the inequalitiesγ\displaystyle\gamma≤(1+1p​α)−1=p​αp​α+1\displaystyle\leq\left(1+\frac{1}{p\alpha}\right)^{-1}=\frac{p\alpha}{p\alpha+1}andλ\displaystyle\lambda>22+1/β​K​(p,α)−γ/p​Cγ/(p​α)​n1−γ/p​H1−β​γ/p\displaystyle>2^{2+1/\beta}K(p,\alpha)^{-\gamma/p}C^{\gamma/(p\alpha)}n^{1-\gamma/p}H^{1-\beta\gamma/p}

are satisfied, thenggis essentially the unique minimizer of the functionalF:BV⁡((x0,xn))→[0,∞)\displaystyle F:\operatorname{BV}\bigl((x_{0},x_{n})\bigr)\to[0,\infty)F​(u)=TV⁡(u)+λ​∥u−g∥pγ,\displaystyle F(u)=\operatorname{TV}(u)+\lambda\lVert u-g\rVert_{p}^{\gamma},

in the sense that any other minimizer coincides withggoutside of the jump set ofgg.

In the course of the proof it would appear natural to rearrange monotonicallyu|Iiu\rvert_{I_{i}}viaSubsection 2.2. Unfortunately, this transformation does not keep track of
jumps at the endpoints of these intervals, which may cause an increase in energy.
This obstruction is overcome by the additional requests on the intervalsIi±I_{i}^{\pm}and a more involved ad hoc proof strategy.

## Remark 3.11.

Notice that the assumption onγ\gammainTheorem 3.4888As a particular case,Theorem 3.1falls in this setting too, withα=log⁡2/log⁡3\alpha=\log 2/\log 3.is complementary to that inTheorem 3.8andTheorem 3.10,
demonstrating the sharpness of our result.

## 4NonlinearTVφ,θ\operatorname{TV}\!\!_{\varphi,\theta}

In this section, we tackle the problem with the nonlinear total variationTVφ,θ\operatorname{TV}\!\!_{\varphi,\theta}. As we will
see in the ensuing sections, this case is remarkably more difficult than the linear case. In
particular, the functional does not behave well with respect to local truncations. To overcome this
difficulty, we study a suitable boundary value problem inSubsection 4.1, characterize its
minimizers, and use them to construct competitors for the problem with the nonlinear total
variation.Subsection 4.2is the nonlinear counterpart toSubsection 3.1, whereas the results inSubsection 3.2cannot be reproduced
in this setting: inSubsection 4.3we proveSubsection 4.3where
we show that different behaviors ofφ\varphiat infinity can disprove bothTheorem 3.8andTheorem 3.10. As a consequence, we propose to study the penalized
functional in (4.7), for which numerical results illustrate the behavior
of minimizers.

## 4.1Boundary value problem

We want to minimize the nonlinear total variation on an interval of a function with prescribed limits
at the endpoints, which can be formulated as the optimization problemE​(L,H)=inf{TVφ,θ⁡(u;(0,L)):u∈BV⁡((0,L)),u+​(0)=0,u−​(L)=H},E(L,H)=\inf\left\{\operatorname{TV}\!\!_{\varphi,\theta}\bigl(u;(0,L)\bigr):u\in\operatorname{BV}\bigl((0,L)\bigr),\ u^{+}(0)=0,\ u^{-}(L)=H\right\},(4.1)

whereL>0L>0andH≥0H\geq 0are two prescribed constants.

## Theorem 4.1.

The problem (4.1) admits at least a minimizeru∈BVreg⁡((0,L))u\in\operatorname{BV}_{\operatorname{reg}}\bigl((0,L)\bigr)of the form999Θ​(⋅)\Theta(\,\cdot\,)is the Heaviside step function.u​(x)=a​x+b​Θ​(x−x¯),u(x)=ax+b\Theta(x-\bar{x}),

for somea,b≥0a,b\geq 0andx¯∈(0,L)\bar{x}\in(0,L), henceE​(L,H)=min⁡{L​φ​(a)+θ​(b):a,b≥0,L​a+b=H}=min⁡{L​φ​(a)+θ​(H−L​a):0≤a≤H/L}=min⁡{L​φ​((H−b)/L)+θ​(b):0≤b≤H}=min⁡{φL​(H​α)+θ​(H​(1−α)):0≤α≤1}.\begin{split}E(L,H)&=\min\left\{L\varphi(a)+\theta(b):a,b\geq 0,\ La+b=H\right\}\\
&=\min\left\{L\varphi(a)+\theta(H-La):0\leq a\leq H/L\right\}\\
&=\min\left\{L\varphi\bigl((H-b)/L\bigr)+\theta(b):0\leq b\leq H\right\}\\
&=\min\left\{\varphi_{L}(H\alpha)+\theta\bigl(H(1-\alpha)\bigr):0\leq\alpha\leq 1\right\}.\end{split}(4.2)

In particular, there is at least one minimizer which has no Cantor part.
Ifφ\varphiis strictly convex andθ\thetais strictly concave then any minimizer is of this form.

As a consequence, the functionu~\tilde{u}such thatD​u~=Da​u​(I)L​ℒ1+Dc​u+Dj​u\mathop{}\!\mathrm{D}\tilde{u}=\frac{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{D}^{a}u(I)}{L}\mathscr{L}^{1}+\mathop{}\!\mathrm{D}^{c}u+\mathop{}\!\mathrm{D}^{j}usatisfiesTVφ,θ⁡(u~)=∫0Lφ(|Da​u​(I)L|)dx+C+∑x∈Juθ(|[u](x)|)≤∫0Lφ​(|u′​(x)|)​d​x+C+∑x∈Juθ​(|[u]​(x)|)=TVφ,θ⁡(u),\begin{split}\operatorname{TV}\!\!_{\varphi,\theta}(\tilde{u})&=\int_{0}^{L}\varphi\mathopen{}\left(\left\lvert\frac{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{D}^{a}u(I)}{L}\right\rvert\right)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x+C+\sum_{x\in J_{u}}\theta\bigl(\lvert[u](x)\rvert\bigr)\\
&\leq\int_{0}^{L}\varphi\bigl(\lvert u^{\prime}(x)\rvert\bigr)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x+C+\sum_{x\in J_{u}}\theta\bigl(\lvert[u](x)\rvert\bigr)=\operatorname{TV}\!\!_{\varphi,\theta}(u),\end{split}

with equality if and only ifu′u^{\prime}is constantly equal toDa​u​(I)/L\mathop{}\!\mathrm{D}^{a}u(I)/L,

Coalescing jumps into a single one.
The functionu~\tilde{u}such thatD​u~=Da​u+Dc​u+Dj​u​(I)​δx¯\mathop{}\!\mathrm{D}\tilde{u}=\mathop{}\!\mathrm{D}^{a}u+\mathop{}\!\mathrm{D}^{c}u+\mathop{}\!\mathrm{D}^{j}u(I)\delta_{\bar{x}}for somex¯∈I\bar{x}\in IsatisfiesTVφ,θ⁡(u~)=∫0Lφ​(|u′​(x)|)​d​x+|Dc​u|​(I)+θ​(|Dj​u​(I)|)=∫0Lφ​(|u′​(x)|)​d​x+|Dc​u|​(I)+θ​(|∑x∈Ju[u]​(x)|)≤∫0Lφ​(|u′​(x)|)​d​x+|Dc​u|​(I)+θ​(∑x∈Ju|[u]​(x)|)≤∫0Lφ​(|u′​(x)|)​d​x+|Dc​u|​(I)+∑x∈Juθ​(|[u]​(x)|)=TVφ,θ⁡(u).\begin{split}\operatorname{TV}\!\!_{\varphi,\theta}(\tilde{u})&=\int_{0}^{L}\varphi\bigl(\lvert u^{\prime}(x)\rvert\bigr)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x+\lvert\mathop{}\!\mathrm{D}^{c}u\rvert(I)+\theta\bigl(\lvert D^{j}u(I)\rvert\bigr)\\
&=\int_{0}^{L}\varphi\bigl(\lvert u^{\prime}(x)\rvert\bigr)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x+\lvert\mathop{}\!\mathrm{D}^{c}u\rvert(I)+\theta\left(\left\lvert\sum_{x\in J_{u}}[u](x)\right\rvert\right)\\
&\leq\int_{0}^{L}\varphi\bigl(\lvert u^{\prime}(x)\rvert\bigr)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x+\lvert\mathop{}\!\mathrm{D}^{c}u\rvert(I)+\theta\left(\sum_{x\in J_{u}}\lvert[u](x)\rvert\right)\\
&\leq\int_{0}^{L}\varphi\bigl(\lvert u^{\prime}(x)\rvert\bigr)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x+\lvert\mathop{}\!\mathrm{D}^{c}u\rvert(I)+\sum_{x\in J_{u}}\theta\bigl(\lvert[u](x)\rvert\bigr)=\operatorname{TV}\!\!_{\varphi,\theta}(u).\end{split}

Let us investigate the equality conditions.
In the first inequality, equality holds if and only if|Dj​u​(I)|=|Dj​u|​(I)\lvert\mathop{}\!\mathrm{D}^{j}u(I)\rvert=\lvert\mathop{}\!\mathrm{D}^{j}u\rvert(I)ifθ\thetais strictly increasing;
in the second inequality, equality holds if and only ifuuhas at most one jump
ifθ\thetais strictly concave.

Ifφ\varphiis strictly convex andθ\thetais strictly concave, then a minimizeruumust necessarily be
non-decreasing, without any Cantor part (Dc​u=0\mathop{}\!\mathrm{D}^{c}u=0), withu′u^{\prime}constant and at most one jump.
Such functions can be written asu​(x)=a​x+b​Θ​(x−x¯)u(x)=ax+b\Theta(x-\bar{x}), whereΘ\Thetais the Heaviside step function,
withx¯∈(0,L)\bar{x}\in(0,L)anda,b∈[0,∞)a,b\in[0,\infty), in which case the functional to minimize isTVφ,θ⁡(u)=L​φ​(a)+θ​(b)\operatorname{TV}\!\!_{\varphi,\theta}(u)=L\varphi(a)+\theta(b).

The admissibility condition for the boundary value problem isa​L+b=HaL+b=H, so we need to finda∈[0,H/L]a\in[0,H/L]that minimizesL​φ​(a)+θ​(H−L​a)L\varphi(a)+\theta(H-La), or equivalentlyb∈[0,H]b\in[0,H]that minimizesL​φ​((H−b)/L)+θ​(b)L\varphi\bigl((H-b)/L\bigr)+\theta(b).
The last formula in (4.2) follows lettingα=LH​a\alpha=\frac{L}{H}a: thena∈[0,H/L]a\in[0,H/L]if and only ifα∈[0,1]\alpha\in[0,1]and we haveL​φ​(a)+θ​(H−L​a)=L​φ​(HL​α)+θ​(H​(1−α))=φL​(H​α)+θ​(H​(1−α)).∎L\varphi(a)+\theta(H-La)=L\varphi\left(\frac{H}{L}\alpha\right)+\theta\bigl(H(1-\alpha)\bigr)=\varphi_{L}(H\alpha)+\theta\bigl(H(1-\alpha)\bigr).\qed

## Remark 4.2.

Ifφ\varphiandθ\thetaare differentiable, then a pair(a,b)(a,b)optimal for (4.2)
and such thata,b>0a,b>0must necessarily satisfyφ′​(a)=θ′​(b)\varphi^{\prime}(a)=\theta^{\prime}(b).
Notice that in general there can be a finite, countable or uncountable number of minimizers of
(4.2) and pairs satisfying the previous condition on the derivative.

## Remark 4.3.

If we extendφ,θ\varphi,\thetatoφ¯,θ¯:ℝ→[0,∞]\bar{\varphi},\bar{\theta}:\mathbb{R}\to[0,\infty]by settingφ¯​(t)=θ¯​(t)=+∞\bar{\varphi}(t)=\bar{\theta}(t)=+\inftyfort<0t<0,131313Notice thatφ¯\bar{\varphi}is still convex, whereasθ¯\bar{\theta}is neither convex
nor concave globally.then (4.2) can be rewritten asE​(L,H)=min0≤b≤H(Lφ(H−bL)+θ(b))=minb≥0(Lφ¯(H−bL)+θ(b))=minb∈ℝ(Lφ¯(H−bL)+θ¯(b))=minb∈ℝ(φ¯L(H−b)+θ¯(b))=(φ¯L□θ¯)(H),\begin{split}E(L,H)&=\min_{0\leq b\leq H}\left(L\varphi\mathopen{}\left(\frac{H-b}{L}\right)+\theta(b)\right)=\min_{b\geq 0}\left(L\bar{\varphi}\mathopen{}\left(\frac{H-b}{L}\right)+\theta(b)\right)\\
&=\min_{b\in\mathbb{R}}\left(L\bar{\varphi}\mathopen{}\left(\frac{H-b}{L}\right)+\bar{\theta}(b)\right)=\min_{b\in\mathbb{R}}\bigl(\bar{\varphi}_{L}(H-b)+\bar{\theta}(b)\bigr)=(\bar{\varphi}_{L}\square\bar{\theta})(H),\end{split}

whereφ¯L​(t)=L​φ¯​(t/L)\bar{\varphi}_{L}(t)=L\bar{\varphi}(t/L)is the perspective ofφ¯\bar{\varphi}and□\squaredenotes theinf\inf-convolution.

In the following propositions we study several properties ofE​(L,H)E(L,H):
monotonicity and subadditivity (Subsection 4.1), upper bounds (Subsection 4.1),
lower bounds (Subsection 4.1), and continuous extension (Subsection 4.1).

## Proposition 4.4.

The following properties are true.
- (i)

E​(L,H)E(L,H)is non-increasing inLLand non-decreasing inHH.
- (ii)

EEis jointly subadditive:E​(L1+L2,H1+H2)≤E​(L1,H1)+E​(L2,H2)E(L_{1}+L_{2},H_{1}+H_{2})\leq E(L_{1},H_{1})+E(L_{2},H_{2}).

## Proof.

We prove the two properties separately.
- (i)

In the last representation formula in (4.2), observe that for everyα∈[0,1]\alpha\in[0,1]the functionφL​(H​α)+θ​(H​(1−α))\varphi_{L}(H\alpha)+\theta\bigl(H(1-\alpha)\bigr)to be minimized
enjoys the claimed monotonicity (the monotonicity inLLfollows from the monotonicity of
the perspectiveφL\varphi_{L}mentioned inSubsection 2.3).

InSubsection A.1we provide two alternative proofs of this property.
- (ii)

Letu1∈BV⁡((0,L1))u_{1}\in\operatorname{BV}((0,L_{1}))andu2∈BV⁡((0,L2))u_{2}\in\operatorname{BV}((0,L_{2}))be optimal forE​(L1,H1)E(L_{1},H_{1})andE​(L2,H2)E(L_{2},H_{2})respectively. Defineu∈BV⁡((0,L1+L2))u\in\operatorname{BV}\bigl((0,L_{1}+L_{2})\bigr)byu​(x)={u1​(x)x∈(0,L1),H1x=L1,H1+u2​(x−L1)x∈(L1,L1+L2).u(x)=\begin{cases}u_{1}(x)&x\in(0,L_{1}),\\
H_{1}&x=L_{1},\\
H_{1}+u_{2}(x-L_{1})&x\in(L_{1},L_{1}+L_{2}).\end{cases}

Observe thatuuis continuous inL1L_{1}and has the correct limits at0andL1+L2L_{1}+L_{2}to be admissible forE​(L1+L2,H1+H2)E(L_{1}+L_{2},H_{1}+H_{2}), thereforeE​(L1+L2,H1+H2)≤TVφ,θ⁡(u,(0,L1+L2))=TVφ,θ⁡(u,(0,L1))+TVφ,θ⁡(u,(L1,L1+L2))=TVφ,θ⁡(u1,(0,L1))+TVφ,θ⁡(u2,(0,L2))=E​(L1,H1)+E​(L2,H2).∎\begin{split}E(L_{1}+L_{2},H_{1}+H_{2})&\leq\operatorname{TV}\!\!_{\varphi,\theta}\bigl(u,(0,L_{1}+L_{2})\bigr)\\
&=\operatorname{TV}\!\!_{\varphi,\theta}\bigl(u,(0,L_{1})\bigr)+\operatorname{TV}\!\!_{\varphi,\theta}\bigl(u,(L_{1},L_{1}+L_{2})\bigr)\\
&=\operatorname{TV}\!\!_{\varphi,\theta}\bigl(u_{1},(0,L_{1})\bigr)+\operatorname{TV}\!\!_{\varphi,\theta}\bigl(u_{2},(0,L_{2})\bigr)\\
&=E(L_{1},H_{1})+E(L_{2},H_{2}).\qed\end{split}
