## Proposition 4.6.

We have the lower boundsE​(L,H)≥φL​(H)+θ​(H)−H,E(L,H)\geq\varphi_{L}(H)+\theta(H)-H,(4.4)

andE​(L,H)≥φL​(H)−(φL′⁣−​(H)​H−θ​(H))+={φL​(H)φL′⁣−​(H)≤θ​(H)/H,φL​(H)−φL′⁣−​(H)​H+θ​(H)φL′⁣−​(H)≥θ​(H)/H,\begin{split}E(L,H)&\geq\varphi_{L}(H)-\bigl(\varphi_{L}^{\prime-}(H)H-\theta(H)\bigr)_{+}\\
&=\begin{cases}\varphi_{L}(H)&\varphi_{L}^{\prime-}(H)\leq\theta(H)/H,\\
\varphi_{L}(H)-\varphi_{L}^{\prime-}(H)H+\theta(H)&\varphi_{L}^{\prime-}(H)\geq\theta(H)/H,\end{cases}\end{split}(4.5)

whereφL′⁣−​(H)=min​∂φL​(H)\varphi_{L}^{\prime-}(H)=\min\partial\varphi_{L}(H)is the smallest element of the subdifferential.141414The estimate is available for every element of the subdifferential, but taking the smallest
leads to the best one.

## Proof.

Let us prove the first lower bound.
Define the complementary functionsψ,ρ:[0,∞)→[0,∞)\psi,\rho:[0,\infty)\to[0,\infty)byψ​(t)=t−φ​(t)\psi(t)=t-\varphi(t)andρ​(t)=t−θ​(t)\rho(t)=t-\theta(t).
Observe thatψ​(0)=ρ​(0)=0\psi(0)=\rho(0)=0,ψ\psiis concave, increasing and sublinear at+∞+\infty,151515In the sense thatlimt→∞ψ​(t)/t=0\lim_{t\to\infty}\psi(t)/t=0.whereasρ\rhois convex andρ′​(0)=0\rho^{\prime}(0)=0, hence increasing too.
If we define alsoψL​(t)=L​ψ​(t/L)\psi_{L}(t)=L\psi(t/L), then we haveψL​(t)=t−φL​(t)\psi_{L}(t)=t-\varphi_{L}(t).
Exploiting the monotonicity ofψL\psi_{L}andρ\rho, for everyα∈[0,1]\alpha\in[0,1]we haveφL​(H​α)+θ​(H​(1−α))=H​α−ψL​(H​α)+H​(1−α)−ρ​(H​(1−α))=H−ψL​(H​α)−ρ​(H​(1−α))≥H−ψL​(H)−ρ​(H)=φL​(H)+θ​(H)−H.\begin{split}\varphi_{L}(H\alpha)+\theta\bigl(H(1-\alpha)\bigr)&=H\alpha-\psi_{L}(H\alpha)+H(1-\alpha)-\rho\bigl(H(1-\alpha)\bigr)\\
&=H-\psi_{L}(H\alpha)-\rho\bigl(H(1-\alpha)\bigr)\\
&\geq H-\psi_{L}(H)-\rho(H)=\varphi_{L}(H)+\theta(H)-H.\end{split}

Let us now prove the second lower bound.
SinceφL\varphi_{L}is convex andθ\thetais concave, for everyt∈[0,H]t\in[0,H]we haveφL​(t)≥φL​(H)+φL′⁣−​(H)​(t−H)\varphi_{L}(t)\geq\varphi_{L}(H)+\varphi_{L}^{\prime-}(H)(t-H)andθ​(t)≥θ​(H)/H​t\theta(t)\geq\theta(H)/Ht, thereforeφL​(H​α)+θ​(H​(1−α))≥φL​(H)+φL′⁣−​(H)​(H​α−H)+θ​(H)​(1−α)≥φL​(H)−(φL′⁣−​(H)​H−θ​(H))​(1−α).\begin{split}\varphi_{L}(H\alpha)+\theta\bigl(H(1-\alpha)\bigr)&\geq\varphi_{L}(H)+\varphi_{L}^{\prime-}(H)(H\alpha-H)+\theta(H)(1-\alpha)\\
&\geq\varphi_{L}(H)-\bigl(\varphi_{L}^{\prime-}(H)H-\theta(H)\bigr)(1-\alpha).\end{split}

IfφL′⁣−​(H)​H≤θ​(H)\varphi_{L}^{\prime-}(H)H\leq\theta(H), the minimum is achieved for1−α=01-\alpha=0and the lower bound is
justφL​(H)\varphi_{L}(H); otherwise it is achieved for1−α=11-\alpha=1, and the lower bound isφL​(H)−φL′⁣−​(H)​H+θ​(H)​(1−α)\varphi_{L}(H)-\varphi_{L}^{\prime-}(H)H+\theta(H)(1-\alpha).
∎

## Remark 4.7.

Ifφ′​(0)=1\varphi^{\prime}(0)=1, then necessarilyφ​(t)=t\varphi(t)=tfor everyt∈[0,∞)t\in[0,\infty).
In (4.5) we always haveθ​(H)/H≤1=φL′​(H)\theta(H)/H\leq 1=\varphi_{L}^{\prime}(H), thereforeE​(L,H)≥θ​(H)E(L,H)\geq\theta(H). Combined with (4.3) we getE​(L,H)=θ​(H)E(L,H)=\theta(H).

If on the other handφ′​(0)<1\varphi^{\prime}(0)<1, sincelimH→0+θ​(H)/H=1\lim_{H\to 0^{+}}\theta(H)/H=1we have that forHHsufficiently smallθ​(H)/H≥φL′⁣−​(H)\theta(H)/H\geq\varphi_{L}^{\prime-}(H), hence from (4.5) we
getE​(L,H)≥φL​(H)E(L,H)\geq\varphi_{L}(H). Combined with (4.3) we getE​(L,H)=φL​(H)E(L,H)=\varphi_{L}(H)forHHsufficiently small.

More in general, one can rewrite the right hand side of the lower bound (4.4) asφL​(H)−ρ​(H)=θ​(H)−ψL​(H)\varphi_{L}(H)-\rho(H)=\theta(H)-\psi_{L}(H). Combined with the upper bounds (4.3),
this leads toφL​(H)−ρ​(H)\displaystyle\varphi_{L}(H)-\rho(H)≤E​(L,H)≤φL​(H),\displaystyle\leq E(L,H)\leq\varphi_{L}(H),θ​(H)−ψL​(H)\displaystyle\theta(H)-\psi_{L}(H)≤E​(L,H)≤θ​(H).\displaystyle\leq E(L,H)\leq\theta(H).

Sinceρ​(H)=o​(H)\rho(H)=o(H)forH→0H\to 0, the former estimate is more useful forHHsmall, whereas the
latter is more useful for large values ofHH, especially whenθ\thetais sublinear.

## Proposition 4.8.

E​(L,H)E(L,H)is continuous in(0,∞)×[0,∞)(0,\infty)\times[0,\infty)and it admits a continuous extension to[0,∞)×[0,∞)[0,\infty)\times[0,\infty), withE​(0,H)=θ​(H)E(0,H)=\theta(H).

## Proof.

Thanks toSubsection 2.3, the functiong​(L,H,α)=φL​(H​α)+θ​(H​(1−α))=φ​(H​α,L)+θ​(H​(1−α))g(L,H,\alpha)=\varphi_{L}(H\alpha)+\theta\bigl(H(1-\alpha)\bigr)=\varphi(H\alpha,L)+\theta\bigl(H(1-\alpha)\bigr)

is continuous in[0,∞)×[0,∞)×[0,1][0,\infty)\times[0,\infty)\times[0,1], hence it is uniformly continuous in[0,L¯]×[0,H¯]×[0,1][0,\bar{L}]\times[0,\bar{H}]\times[0,1]for everyL¯,H¯>0\bar{L},\bar{H}>0. From this we deduce that
in(0,L¯)×[0,H¯)(0,\bar{L})\times[0,\bar{H})the functionE​(L,H)=minα∈[0,1]⁡g​(L,H,α)E(L,H)=\min_{\alpha\in[0,1]}g(L,H,\alpha)

is continuous.
Recalling thatφ′​(∞)=1\varphi^{\prime}(\infty)=1and thereforeφ​(H,0)=H\varphi(H,0)=H,
the continuous extension toL=0L=0is provided by the minimization problem in the right hand side,
i.e.minα∈[0,1]⁡g​(0,H,α)=minα∈[0,1]⁡[φ​(H​α,0)+θ​(H​(1−α))]=minα∈[0,1]⁡[H​α+θ​(H​(1−α))]=minα∈[0,1]⁡[H−ρ​(H​(1−α))]=H−ρ​(H)=θ​(H).∎\begin{split}\min_{\alpha\in[0,1]}g(0,H,\alpha)&=\min_{\alpha\in[0,1]}\bigl[\varphi(H\alpha,0)+\theta\bigl(H(1-\alpha)\bigr)\bigr]=\min_{\alpha\in[0,1]}\bigl[H\alpha+\theta\bigl(H(1-\alpha)\bigr)\bigr]\\
&=\min_{\alpha\in[0,1]}\bigl[H-\rho\bigl(H(1-\alpha)\bigr)\bigr]=H-\rho(H)=\theta(H).\qed\end{split}

## 4.2The input is not a minimizer

Recall fromSubsection 3.1that the Cantor function provides a simple example
where the input is not a minimizer even in the nonlinear setting.
This is generalized in the following theorem to an input datumggwith at leastα\alpha-growth
at one endpoint, providing the nonlinear analogue ofTheorem 3.4.

## Theorem 4.9.

Letg∈BVreg⁡((a,b))g\in\operatorname{BV}_{\operatorname{reg}}\bigl((a,b)\bigr)be a function with the property that there existα,c>0\alpha,c>0and0<δ<b−a0<\delta<b-asuch thatg​(x)−g+​(a)≥c​(x−a)αg(x)-g^{+}(a)\geq c(x-a)^{\alpha}for allx∈(a,a+δ]x\in(a,a+\delta].
Assume that there existC,η>0C,\eta>0such thatE​(δ,H)≥C​HηE(\delta,H)\geq CH^{\eta}for everyH∈[0,H¯]H\in[0,\bar{H}],
whereH¯=ess​sup(a,a+δ]⁡g−g+​(a)\bar{H}=\operatorname*{ess\,sup}_{(a,a+\delta]}g-g^{+}(a).
Ifγ>η​(1+1p​α)−1=p​α​ηp​α+1\gamma>\eta\left(1+\frac{1}{p\alpha}\right)^{-1}=\frac{p\alpha\eta}{p\alpha+1}

thenggis not a minimizer of the functionalF​(u)=TVφ,θ⁡(u)+λ​∥u−g∥pγF(u)=\operatorname{TV}\!\!_{\varphi,\theta}(u)+\lambda\lVert u-g\rVert_{p}^{\gamma}

for anyp∈[1,∞]p\in[1,\infty]andλ>0\lambda>0.

## Proof.

Define the non-decreasing functionω:(a,a+δ]→ℝ\omega:(a,a+\delta]\to\mathbb{R}ω​(x)=ess​sup(a,x]⁡g\omega(x)=\operatorname*{ess\,sup}_{(a,x]}g

and observe that we haveω​(x)≥g+​(a)+c​(x−a)α\omega(x)\geq g^{+}(a)+c(x-a)^{\alpha}andω+​(a)=g+​(a)\omega^{+}(a)=g^{+}(a).
Giveny∈(a,a+δ]y\in(a,a+\delta], introduce the auxiliary parameterh=h​(y)=ω​(y)−g+​(a)≥c​(y−a)α>0h=h(y)=\omega(y)-g^{+}(a)\geq c(y-a)^{\alpha}>0.

For everyε>0\varepsilon>0, the set{x∈(a,y]:g​(x)≥ω​(y)−ε}\{x\in(a,y]:g(x)\geq\omega(y)-\varepsilon\}has positiveℒ1\mathscr{L}^{1}-measure,
andℒ1\mathscr{L}^{1}-a.e.x∈(a,y]x\in(a,y]is a continuity point ofgg, therefore there existsxy,ε∈(a,y]x_{y,\varepsilon}\in(a,y]such thatg​(xy,ε)≥ω​(y)−εg(x_{y,\varepsilon})\geq\omega(y)-\varepsilonandggis continuous inxy,εx_{y,\varepsilon}.
From this continuity, we deduceg​(xy,ε)≤ω​(y)g(x_{y,\varepsilon})\leq\omega(y), hence|ω​(y)−g​(xy,ε)|≤ε\lvert\omega(y)-g(x_{y,\varepsilon})\rvert\leq\varepsilon.
Define the truncated functionuy,ε∈BV⁡((a,b))u_{y,\varepsilon}\in\operatorname{BV}\bigl((a,b)\bigr)uy,ε​(x)={ω​(y)x∈(a,xy,ε),g​(x)x∈[xy,ε,b).u_{y,\varepsilon}(x)=\begin{cases}\omega(y)&x\in(a,x_{y,\varepsilon}),\\
g(x)&x\in[x_{y,\varepsilon},b).\end{cases}

We haveTVφ,θ⁡(uy,ε;(a,b))=TVφ,θ⁡(uy,ε;(a,xy,ε))+θ​(|uy,ε−​(xy,ε)−uy,ε+​(xy,ε)|)+TVφ,θ⁡(uy,ε;(xy,ε,b))=θ​(|ω​(y)−g​(xy,ε)|)+TVφ,θ⁡(g;(xy,ε,b))≤θ​(ε)+TVφ,θ⁡(g;(xy,ε,b)).\begin{split}\operatorname{TV}\!\!_{\varphi,\theta}\bigl(u_{y,\varepsilon};(a,b)\bigr)&=\operatorname{TV}\!\!_{\varphi,\theta}\bigl(u_{y,\varepsilon};(a,x_{y,\varepsilon})\bigr)+\theta(\lvert u_{y,\varepsilon}^{-}(x_{y,\varepsilon})-u_{y,\varepsilon}^{+}(x_{y,\varepsilon})\rvert)+\operatorname{TV}\!\!_{\varphi,\theta}\bigl(u_{y,\varepsilon};(x_{y,\varepsilon},b)\bigr)\\
&=\theta\bigl(\lvert\omega(y)-g(x_{y,\varepsilon})\rvert\bigr)+\operatorname{TV}\!\!_{\varphi,\theta}\bigl(g;(x_{y,\varepsilon},b)\bigr)\\
&\leq\theta(\varepsilon)+\operatorname{TV}\!\!_{\varphi,\theta}\bigl(g;(x_{y,\varepsilon},b)\bigr).\end{split}

On the other hand, recalling the continuity ofgginxy,εx_{y,\varepsilon},xy,ε−a≤y−a≤δx_{y,\varepsilon}-a\leq y-a\leq\delta,
the monotonicity stated inSubsection 4.1, and the lower bound forE​(δ,⋅)E(\delta,\,\cdot\,),
we haveTVφ,θ⁡(g;(a,b))=TVφ,θ⁡(g;(a,xy,ε))+TVφ,θ⁡(g;(xy,ε,b))≥E​(xy,ε−a,|g​(xy,ε)−g+​(a)|)+TVφ,θ⁡(g;(xy,ε,b))≥E​(δ,|ω​(y)−g+​(a)|−ε)+TVφ,θ⁡(g;(xy,ε,b))=E​(δ,h−ε)+TVφ,θ⁡(g;(xy,ε,b))≥C​(h−ε)η+TVφ,θ⁡(g;(xy,ε,b)).\begin{split}\operatorname{TV}\!\!_{\varphi,\theta}\bigl(g;(a,b)\bigr)&=\operatorname{TV}\!\!_{\varphi,\theta}\bigl(g;(a,x_{y,\varepsilon})\bigr)+\operatorname{TV}\!\!_{\varphi,\theta}\bigl(g;(x_{y,\varepsilon},b)\bigr)\\
&\geq E\bigl(x_{y,\varepsilon}-a,\lvert g(x_{y,\varepsilon})-g^{+}(a)\rvert\bigr)+\operatorname{TV}\!\!_{\varphi,\theta}\bigl(g;(x_{y,\varepsilon},b)\bigr)\\
&\geq E\bigl(\delta,\lvert\omega(y)-g^{+}(a)\rvert-\varepsilon\bigr)+\operatorname{TV}\!\!_{\varphi,\theta}\bigl(g;(x_{y,\varepsilon},b)\bigr)\\
&=E\bigl(\delta,h-\varepsilon\bigr)+\operatorname{TV}\!\!_{\varphi,\theta}\bigl(g;(x_{y,\varepsilon},b)\bigr)\\
&\geq C(h-\varepsilon)^{\eta}+\operatorname{TV}\!\!_{\varphi,\theta}\bigl(g;(x_{y,\varepsilon},b)\bigr).\end{split}

From these two total variation estimates we getTVφ,θ⁡(g;(a,b))≥TVφ,θ⁡(uy,ε;(a,b))+C​(h−ε)η−θ​(ε).\operatorname{TV}\!\!_{\varphi,\theta}\bigl(g;(a,b)\bigr)\geq\operatorname{TV}\!\!_{\varphi,\theta}\bigl(u_{y,\varepsilon};(a,b)\bigr)+C(h-\varepsilon)^{\eta}-\theta(\varepsilon).

Since forℒ1\mathscr{L}^{1}-a.e.x∈(a,a+δ]x\in(a,a+\delta]we haveuy,ε​(x)≥g​(x)u_{y,\varepsilon}(x)\geq g(x),
with a similar computation as inTheorem 3.4, we have∥uy,ε−g∥Lp​((a,b))p≤K​(p,α)​C−1/α​hp+1/α.\lVert u_{y,\varepsilon}-g\rVert_{L^{p}((a,b))}^{p}\leq K(p,\alpha)C^{-1/\alpha}h^{p+1/\alpha}.

Combining the estimates for the total variation and the fidelity term we arrive atF​(uy,ε)−F​(g)≤θ​(ε)−C​(h−ε)η+λ​K​(p,α)γ/p​c−γ/(p​α)​hγ​(1+1p​α),F(u_{y,\varepsilon})-F(g)\leq\theta(\varepsilon)-C(h-\varepsilon)^{\eta}+\lambda K(p,\alpha)^{\gamma/p}c^{-\gamma/(p\alpha)}h^{\gamma\bigl(1+\frac{1}{p\alpha}\bigr)},

which can be made strictly negative under the assumption on the exponentγ\gammasendingε→0\varepsilon\to 0andy→0y\to 0, which impliesh​(y)→0h(y)\to 0.
∎

Observe thatSubsection 4.1provides a convenient way to verify the assumption onE​(δ,⋅)E(\delta,\,\cdot\,).
For instance, ifφ​(t)=c​tη+o​(tη)\varphi(t)=ct^{\eta}+o(t^{\eta})fort→0t\to 0withη>1\eta>1, then bySubsection 4.1we haveE​(L,H)=φL​(H)=c​L1−η​Hη+o​(Hη)E(L,H)=\varphi_{L}(H)=cL^{1-\eta}H^{\eta}+o(H^{\eta})forH→0H\to 0, hence
the assumption is satisfied.

## 4.3Is the input a minimizer?

The intent of this section is to try and replicate the results contained inSubsection 3.2.
Unfortunately, as it turns out, the expected statements are not true. Indeed, the culprit lies in
the failure of the nonlinear analogue ofSubsection 3.2: more specifically, while
it would be possible to replicate the majority of its proof, the sub-case(ii)(ii)()proves to be an insurmountable obstruction, due to the fact that not all monotone functions are minimizers of the boundary value problem forTVφ,θ\operatorname{TV}\!\!_{\varphi,\theta}, a simple fact that was exploited in(ii)(ii)()to perform the local truncation without degrading the linear total variation.

Case(i)ofSubsection 4.3demonstrates a
simple situation where the nonlinear analogues ofSubsection 3.2andTheorem 3.10both fail, exploiting the behavior at infinity of the
nonlinearityφ\varphi, see (4.6a). The sharpness of the assumption is highlighted
by the opposite behavior under the complementary conditions in case(ii)of the same proposition. In the particular casep=γp=\gamma, conditions (4.6) amount to the existence or non-existence of an
oblique asymptote forφ\varphi; in the caseγ>p\gamma>p, they are related to a sufficiently fast
convergence rate to the oblique asymptote.

A more detailed analysis of(ii)(ii)()leads us to consider the penalized problem inSubsection 4.3, which reveals the underlying reason for the failure of the
desired nonlinear statements: constant functions are not optimal profiles for transitioning between
two levels, not even in the presence of a fidelity term with respect to a constant datum.

## Proposition 4.10.

LetL,H>0L,H>0. Letg∈BV⁡((0,L))g\in\operatorname{BV}\bigl((0,L)\bigr)given byg​(x)=H​Θ​(x−x0)={0x<x0,Hx>x0,g(x)=H\Theta(x-x_{0})=\begin{cases}0&x<x_{0},\\

Ifθ​(H)=H\theta(H)=Handinfα≥α∗φ​(α)−αα1−γ/p=−∞,\inf_{\alpha\geq\alpha^{*}}\frac{\varphi(\alpha)-\alpha}{\alpha^{1-\gamma/p}}=-\infty,(4.6a)

then for everyλ≥0\lambda\geq 0there existsα≥α∗\alpha\geq\alpha^{*}such thatF​(uα)<F​(g)F(u_{\alpha})<F(g);
hence the inputggis not optimal.

To replicate the truncation argument used in(ii)(ii)(), we would need the zero function to be a

among all functionsu∈B​V​((0,L))u\in BV\bigl((0,L)\bigr)withu+​(0)=0u^{+}(0)=0.

finallyuumust be convex since the increasing rearrangement ofu′u^{\prime}does not change the total
variation but lowers the fidelity.

## Proposition 4.11.

LetL,H>0L,H>0, letp,γ≥1p,\gamma\geq 1, and assumeθ−′​(H)>φ+′​(0)\theta^{\prime}_{-}(H)>\varphi^{\prime}_{+}(0).
Then the functiong=0g=0is not a local minimizer inBV⁡((0,L))\operatorname{BV}\bigl((0,L)\bigr)of the functionalF​(u)=TVφ,θ⁡(u;(0,L))+θ​(|H−u−​(L)|)+λ​∥u∥pγF(u)=\operatorname{TV}\!\!_{\varphi,\theta}\bigl(u;(0,L)\bigr)+\theta\bigl(\lvert H-u^{-}(L)\rvert\bigr)+\lambda\lVert u\rVert_{p}^{\gamma}

with boundary conditionu+​(0)=0u^{+}(0)=0for anyλ>0\lambda>0.

## Proof.

Considering non-negative variationsv∈W1,1​((0,L);[0,∞))v\in W^{1,1}\bigl((0,L);[0,\infty)\bigr)withv+​(0)=0v^{+}(0)=0,
the one-sided directional derivative of the functionalFFatu=gu=gis (seeAppendix B)limε→0+F​(g+ε​v)−F​(g)ε=∫0Lφ+′​(0)​v′​(x)​d​x−θ−′​(H)​v−​(L)+δγ,1​λ​∥v∥p=(φ+′​(0)−θ−′​(H))​v−​(L)+δγ,1​λ​∥v∥p.\begin{split}\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0^{+}}\frac{F(g+\varepsilon v)-F(g)}{\varepsilon}&=\int_{0}^{L}\varphi^{\prime}_{+}(0)v^{\prime}(x)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x-\theta^{\prime}_{-}(H)v^{-}(L)+\delta_{\gamma,1}\lambda\lVert v\rVert_{p}\\
&=\bigl(\varphi^{\prime}_{+}(0)-\theta^{\prime}_{-}(H)\bigr)v^{-}(L)+\delta_{\gamma,1}\lambda\lVert v\rVert_{p}.\end{split}

For anyλ\lambda, this can be made strictly negative by takingv​(x)=(x/L)qv(x)=(x/L)^{q}withq>0q>0large enough.
This disproves the local minimality ofg∈W1,1​((0,L))g\in W^{1,1}\bigl((0,L)\bigr), hence inB​V​((0,L))BV\bigl((0,L)\bigr).
∎

## 4.3.1Numerical examples

We conclude this section by investigating numerically the nature of the global minimizer in a simple
situation. Let us takep=γ=1p=\gamma=1andφ,θ\varphi,\thetadifferentiable.
Recall that the minimizer is a convex functionu∈W1,1​((0,L);[0,H])u\in W^{1,1}\bigl((0,L);[0,H]\bigr)withu+​(0)=0u^{+}(0)=0.
Letx0=max{x∈[0,L]:u|(0,x]=0}x_{0}=\max\{x\in[0,L]:u\rvert_{(0,x]}=0\}.
FromSubsection B.1.1we know that in the interval(x0,L)(x_{0},L)the optimal profileuuis given by (B.7):u​(x)={0x∈(0,x0],1λ​φ∗​(φ′​(0)+λ​(x−x0))x∈(x0,L).u(x)=\begin{cases}0&x\in(0,x_{0}],\\
\frac{1}{\lambda}\varphi^{*}\bigl(\varphi^{\prime}(0)+\lambda(x-x_{0})\bigr)&x\in(x_{0},L).\end{cases}

In this sub section, we consider two explicit examples:φ1​(t)\displaystyle\varphi_{1}(t)=t−log⁡(1+t),\displaystyle=t-\log(1+t),φ2​(t)\displaystyle\varphi_{2}(t)=1+t2−1,\displaystyle=\sqrt{1+t^{2}}-1,θ1​(t)\displaystyle\theta_{1}(t)=log⁡(1+t),\displaystyle=\log(1+t),θ2​(t)\displaystyle\theta_{2}(t)=1+2​t−1.\displaystyle=\sqrt{1+2t}-1.

For the non-linearityφ1\varphi_{1}we haveφ1′​(0)=0\varphi_{1}^{\prime}(0)=0andφ1∗​(τ)=−τ−log⁡(1−τ)\varphi_{1}^{*}(\tau)=-\tau-\log(1-\tau),
therefore in the interval(x0,L)(x_{0},L)u​(x)=−(x−x0)−log⁡(1−λ​(x−x0))λ.u(x)=-(x-x_{0})-\frac{\log\bigl(1-\lambda(x-x_{0})\bigr)}{\lambda}.

On the other hand, forφ2\varphi_{2}we haveφ2′​(0)=0\varphi_{2}^{\prime}(0)=0andφ2∗​(τ)=1−1−τ2\varphi_{2}^{*}(\tau)=1-\sqrt{1-\tau^{2}},
hence in the interval(x0,L)(x_{0},L)u​(x)=1−1−λ2​(x−x0)2λ.u(x)=\frac{1-\sqrt{1-\lambda^{2}(x-x_{0})^{2}}}{\lambda}.

## Numerical implementation

The numerical approximations of the optimal profiles are obtained as follows.
Knowing that the minimizer of the functionalFFshould be a non-negative continuous function161616Additionally,u∈W1,1​((0,L);[0,H])u\in W^{1,1}\bigl((0,L);[0,H]\bigr)non-decreasing and convex.,
for a fixedn∈ℕ+n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}we introduce the uniform meshxi=i​Δ​xx_{i}=i\Delta x, whereΔ​x=L/n\Delta x=L/n,
and approximate the unknown functionuuwith a continuous piecewise affine functionu^​(x)=∑i=1n(xi−xxi−xi−1​ui−1+x−xi−1xi−xi−1​ui)​𝟏(xi−1,xi]​(x)\hat{u}(x)=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\frac{x_{i}-x}{x_{i}-x_{i-1}}u_{i-1}+\frac{x-x_{i-1}}{x_{i}-x_{i-1}}u_{i}\right)\bm{1}_{(x_{i-1},x_{i}]}(x)

parametrized by the vector(u1,…,un)∈[0,∞)n(u_{1},\dots,u_{n})\in[0,\infty)^{n}, with the conventionu0=0u_{0}=0.
The valuesui=u^​(xi)u_{i}=\hat{u}(x_{i})correspond to a sampling ofuuover the uniform mesh.
This leads to introducing the functionFn:[0,∞)n→[0,∞)F_{n}:[0,\infty)^{n}\to[0,\infty)given byFn​(u1,…,un)=F​(u^)=∑i=1nφ​(|ui−ui−1Δ​x|)​Δ​x+θ​(|H−un|)+λ​(∑i=1n−1ui​Δ​x+un2​Δ​x)=(∑i=1nφ​(|ui−ui−1Δ​x|)+λ​(∑i=1n−1ui+un2))​Δ​x+θ​(|H−un|),\begin{split}F_{n}(u_{1},\dots,u_{n})&=F(\hat{u})=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\varphi\left(\left\lvert\frac{u_{i}-u_{i-1}}{\Delta x}\right\rvert\right)\Delta x+\theta(\lvert H-u_{n}\rvert)+\lambda\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}u_{i}\Delta x+\frac{u_{n}}{2}\Delta x\right)\\
&=\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\varphi\left(\left\lvert\frac{u_{i}-u_{i-1}}{\Delta x}\right\rvert\right)+\lambda\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}u_{i}+\frac{u_{n}}{2}\right)\right)\Delta x+\theta(\lvert H-u_{n}\rvert),\end{split}

which we minimize among the vectors(u1,…,un)∈[0,H]n(u_{1},\dots,u_{n})\in[0,H]^{n}.
The functionFnF_{n}amounts to approximating theL1L^{1}norm in the fidelity with the trapezoidal rule,
the derivativeu′u^{\prime}with finite differences, and the integral ofφ​(|u′|)\varphi(\lvert u^{\prime}\rvert)with the rectangle rule.

Alternatively, the problem can be reformulated in terms of the discrete slopes(a1,…,an)∈ℝn(a_{1},\dots,a_{n})\in\mathbb{R}^{n}, which are related to the values(u1,…,un)∈ℝn(u_{1},\dots,u_{n})\in\mathbb{R}^{n}by the
transformationai=ui−ui−1Δ​x,ui=∑j=1iai​Δ​x.a_{i}=\frac{u_{i}-u_{i-1}}{\Delta x},\qquad u_{i}=\sum_{j=1}^{i}a_{i}\Delta x.

Notice that the mappingℝn→ℝn\mathbb{R}^{n}\to\mathbb{R}^{n}between(ui)i=1n(u_{i})_{i=1}^{n}and(ai)i=1n(a_{i})_{i=1}^{n}is a bijection.
Recalling the monotonicity of the optimal profileuu, the slopes(ai)i=1n(a_{i})_{i=1}^{n}can be assumed to
be non-negative.

The functionGn:[0,∞)n→[0,∞)G_{n}:[0,\infty)^{n}\to[0,\infty)which corresponds toFnF_{n}in this set of coordinates,
i.e.Gn​(a1,…,an)=Fn​(u1,…,un)G_{n}(a_{1},\dots,a_{n})=F_{n}(u_{1},\dots,u_{n}), is given byGn​(a1,…,an)=Δx(∑i=1nφ(ai)+λΔx(∑i=1n−1∑j=1iaj+12∑j=1naj))+θ(|H−Δx∑j=1naj|)=Δx(∑i=1nφ(ai)+λΔx(∑i=1nai(n−i+1/2)))+θ(|H−Δx∑j=1naj|).\begin{split}&G_{n}(a_{1},\dots,a_{n})\\
&=\Delta x\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\varphi(a_{i})+\lambda\Delta x\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}\sum_{j=1}^{i}a_{j}+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=1}^{n}a_{j}\right)\right)+\theta\mathopen{}\left(\left\lvert H-\Delta x\sum_{j=1}^{n}a_{j}\right\rvert\right)\\
&=\Delta x\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\varphi(a_{i})+\lambda\Delta x\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}a_{i}(n-i+1/2)\right)\right)+\theta\mathopen{}\left(\left\lvert H-\Delta x\sum_{j=1}^{n}a_{j}\right\rvert\right).\end{split}

Not only the formulation in terms of the slopes avoids the bad conditioning arising from the finite
difference operator, but it also allows to easily restrict the search to monotone functions instead
of merely non-negative ones. These benefits manifest empirically in a faster convergence of the
optimization algorithm.

The actual numerical minimization has been implemented in the Julia programming language[4]using the box-constrained limited-memory Broyden–Fletcher–Goldfarb–Shanno (L-BFGS-B) algorithm
provided by the packageOptim.jl[9]. In addition, a multistart strategy from
some hand-picked and some random initial points has been employed in order to reduce the risk of
converging to a local minimum.

The approximate profiles obtained via minimization ofGnG_{n}have been validated by comparison with
the semi-analytical approach described inSubsection B.1.1, which consists
in minimizing the scalar function defined in (B.9) subjected to the constraint
(B.10).

As an example, inFigure 6we plot the functionffdefined in (B.9)
associated with non-linearitiesφ1,θ1\varphi_{1},\theta_{1}and parametersL=H=1L=H=1,λ=1.1\lambda=1.1; the minimum
is found atx0≈0.4801305x_{0}\approx 0.4801305, as already mentioned inFigure 5.

non-linearitiesφ1,θ1\varphi_{1},\theta_{1}and parametersL=H=1L=H=1,λ=1.1\lambda=1.1. The same plot is
presented at two different vertical zoom levels. The vertical dashed lines, from left to right,
are positioned atL−1−φ′​(0)λ≈0.0909091L-\frac{1-\varphi^{\prime}(0)}{\lambda}\approx 0.0909091andL−(φ∗)−1​(λ​H)−φ′​(0)λ≈0.21909L-\frac{(\varphi^{*})^{-1}(\lambda H)-\varphi^{\prime}(0)}{\lambda}\approx 0.21909.
The minimum, highlighted with a black dot, is found atx0≈0.4801305x_{0}\approx 0.4801305, as already
mentioned inFigure 5.Figure 7:plot of the functionffdefined in (B.9) associated with

and|Dj​v|\lvert\mathop{}\!\mathrm{D}^{j}v\rvertand|Dj​u|\lvert\mathop{}\!\mathrm{D}^{j}u\rverthave the same atom values.
On the other hand, sinceL1/L2≤1L_{1}/L_{2}\leq 1, we haveφ​((L1/L2)​t)≤(L1/L2)​φ​(t)\varphi\bigl((L_{1}/L_{2})t\bigr)\leq(L_{1}/L_{2})\varphi(t)for everyt≥0t\geq 0, hence∫0L2φ​(v′​(x))​d​x=∫0L2φ(L1L2u′(L1L2x))dx≤∫0L2L1L2φ(u′(L1L2x))dx=∫0L1φ(u′(x))dx\begin{split}\int_{0}^{L_{2}}\varphi\bigl(v^{\prime}(x)\bigr)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x&=\int_{0}^{L_{2}}\varphi\mathopen{}\left(\frac{L_{1}}{L_{2}}u^{\prime}\mathopen{}\left(\frac{L_{1}}{L_{2}}x\right)\right)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\\

## Alternative proof ofSubsection 4.1(i), from (4.2).

## Appendix BFirst variation of the functional inSubsection 4.3

Letσ:[0,∞)→ℝ\sigma:[0,\infty\bigr)\to\mathbb{R}be a function which admits left and right derivatives at every point.
We introduce the following notation for the one-sided directional derivative of the compositionσ∘|⋅|\sigma\circ\lvert\,\cdot\,\rvert:𝒟σ​(a)​[b]≔(σ∘|⋅|)sign⁡b′​(a)​b=limε→0+σ​(|a+ε​b|)−σ​(|a|)ε={σ+′​(0)​|b|a=0,σsign⁡(a​b)′​(|a|)​sign⁡(a)​ba≠0,\mathcal{D}_{\sigma}(a)[b]\coloneqq(\sigma\circ\lvert\,\cdot\,\rvert)^{\prime}_{\operatorname{sign}b}(a)b=\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0^{+}}\frac{\sigma(\lvert a+\varepsilon b\rvert)-\sigma(\lvert a\rvert)}{\varepsilon}=\begin{cases}\sigma^{\prime}_{+}(0)\lvert b\rvert&a=0,\\
\sigma^{\prime}_{\operatorname{sign}(ab)}(\lvert a\rvert)\operatorname{sign}(a)b&a\neq 0,\end{cases}

whereσ−′,σ+′\sigma^{\prime}_{-},\sigma^{\prime}_{+}denote the left and right derivatives respectively.
Notice that, for every fixeda∈ℝa\in\mathbb{R}, the function𝒟σ​(a)​[b]\mathcal{D}_{\sigma}(a)[b]is positively
1-homogeneous inbb.
Moreover, ifσ\sigmais differentiable everywhere, then𝒟σ​(a)​[b]=σ′​(|a|)​sign⁡(a)​b\mathcal{D}_{\sigma}(a)[b]=\sigma^{\prime}(\lvert a\rvert)\operatorname{sign}(a)bis linear inbbfora≠0a\neq 0, and it is linear fora=0a=0if and only ifσ+′​(0)=0\sigma^{\prime}_{+}(0)=0.

Let us compute the one-sided Gâteaux derivative of the functionalFF.
Starting with the total variation, foru,v∈W1,1​((0,L))u,v\in W^{1,1}\bigl((0,L)\bigr)we havelimε→0+TVφ,θ⁡(u+ε​v)−TVφ,θ⁡(u)ε=∫0Llimε→0+φ​(|u′​(x)+ε​v′​(x)|)−φ​(|u′​(x)|)ε​d​x=∫0L𝒟φ​(u′​(x))​[v′​(x)]​d​x;\begin{split}\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0^{+}}\frac{\operatorname{TV}\!\!_{\varphi,\theta}(u+\varepsilon v)-\operatorname{TV}\!\!_{\varphi,\theta}(u)}{\varepsilon}&=\int_{0}^{L}\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0^{+}}\frac{\varphi\bigl(\lvert u^{\prime}(x)+\varepsilon v^{\prime}(x)\rvert\bigr)-\varphi\bigl(\lvert u^{\prime}(x)\rvert\bigr)}{\varepsilon}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\\
&=\int_{0}^{L}\mathcal{D}_{\varphi}\bigl(u^{\prime}(x)\bigr)[v^{\prime}(x)]\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x;\end{split}

on the other hand, the derivative of the penalization at the right endpoint islimε→0+θ​(|u−​(L)+ε​v−​(L)−H|)−θ​(|u−​(L)−H|)ε=𝒟θ​(u−​(L)−H)​[v−​(L)].\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0^{+}}\frac{\theta\bigl(\lvert u^{-}(L)+\varepsilon v^{-}(L)-H\rvert\bigr)-\theta\bigl(\lvert u^{-}(L)-H\rvert\bigr)}{\varepsilon}=\mathcal{D}_{\theta}\bigl(u^{-}(L)-H\bigr)[v^{-}(L)].

Let us now compute the one-sided Gâteaux derivative of∥u∥pγ\lVert u\rVert_{p}^{\gamma}.
Whenu=0u=0is the identically zero function, we havelimε→0+∥u+ε​v∥pγ−∥u∥pγε=limε→0+εγε​∥v∥pγ={0γ>1,∥v∥pγ=1,∞γ<1.\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0^{+}}\frac{\lVert u+\varepsilon v\rVert_{p}^{\gamma}-\lVert u\rVert_{p}^{\gamma}}{\varepsilon}=\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0^{+}}\frac{\varepsilon^{\gamma}}{\varepsilon}\lVert v\rVert_{p}^{\gamma}=\begin{cases}0&\gamma>1,\\
\lVert v\rVert_{p}&\gamma=1,\\
\infty&\gamma<1.\end{cases}

Assume nowu≢0u\not\equiv 0, hence∥u∥p>0\lVert u\rVert_{p}>0.
Forp=1p=1we havelimε→0+∥u+ε​v∥pp−∥u∥ppε=limε→0+∫0L|u​(x)+ε​v​(x)|−|u​(x)|ε​d​x=∫{u≠0}sign⁡u​(x)​v​(x)​d​x+∫{u=0}|v​(x)|​d​x,\begin{split}\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0^{+}}\frac{\lVert u+\varepsilon v\rVert_{p}^{p}-\lVert u\rVert_{p}^{p}}{\varepsilon}&=\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0^{+}}\int_{0}^{L}\frac{\lvert u(x)+\varepsilon v(x)\rvert-\lvert u(x)\rvert}{\varepsilon}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\\
&=\int_{\{u\neq 0\}}\operatorname{sign}u(x)v(x)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\{u=0\}}\lvert v(x)\rvert\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x,\end{split}

whereas forp>1p>1we havelimε→0+∥u+ε​v∥pp−∥u∥ppε=limε→0+∫0L|u​(x)+ε​v​(x)|p−|u​(x)|pε​d​x=∫0Lp​|u​(x)|p−1​sign⁡u​(x)​v​(x)​d​x=∫{u≠0}p​|u​(x)|p−1​sign⁡u​(x)​v​(x)​d​x.\begin{split}\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0^{+}}\frac{\lVert u+\varepsilon v\rVert_{p}^{p}-\lVert u\rVert_{p}^{p}}{\varepsilon}&=\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0^{+}}\int_{0}^{L}\frac{\lvert u(x)+\varepsilon v(x)\rvert^{p}-\lvert u(x)\rvert^{p}}{\varepsilon}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\\
&=\int_{0}^{L}p\lvert u(x)\rvert^{p-1}\operatorname{sign}u(x)v(x)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\\
&=\int_{\{u\neq 0\}}p\lvert u(x)\rvert^{p-1}\operatorname{sign}u(x)v(x)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x.\end{split}

These last two formulas can be combined succinctly aslimε→0+∥u+ε​v∥pp−∥u∥ppε=∫{u≠0}|u​(x)|p−1​sign⁡u​(x)​v​(x)​d​x+δp,1​∫{u=0}|v​(x)|​d​x,\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0^{+}}\frac{\lVert u+\varepsilon v\rVert_{p}^{p}-\lVert u\rVert_{p}^{p}}{\varepsilon}=\int_{\{u\neq 0\}}\lvert u(x)\rvert^{p-1}\operatorname{sign}u(x)v(x)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x+\delta_{p,1}\int_{\{u=0\}}\lvert v(x)\rvert\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x,(B.1)

valid for allp≥1p\geq 1, whereδp,1\delta_{p,1}denotes the Kronecker delta.
Writing∥u∥pγ=(∥u∥pp)γ/p\lVert u\rVert_{p}^{\gamma}=(\lVert u\rVert_{p}^{p})^{\gamma/p}, by the chain rule we finally getlimε→0+∥u+ε​v∥pγ−∥u∥pγε=γp​∥u∥pγ−p​limε→0+∥u+ε​v∥pp−∥u∥ppε,\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0^{+}}\frac{\lVert u+\varepsilon v\rVert_{p}^{\gamma}-\lVert u\rVert_{p}^{\gamma}}{\varepsilon}=\frac{\gamma}{p}\lVert u\rVert_{p}^{\gamma-p}\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0^{+}}\frac{\lVert u+\varepsilon v\rVert_{p}^{p}-\lVert u\rVert_{p}^{p}}{\varepsilon},

where this latter right derivative is given by (B.1).

In conclusion, the first variation of the functionalFFat the identically zero functionu=0u=0islimε→0+F​(0+ε​v)−F​(0)ε=∫0Lφ+′​(0)​|v′​(x)|​d​x+θsign⁡v−​(L)′​(H)​v−​(L)+{0γ>1,λ​∥v∥pγ=1,∞γ<1;\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0^{+}}\frac{F(0+\varepsilon v)-F(0)}{\varepsilon}=\int_{0}^{L}\varphi^{\prime}_{+}(0)\lvert v^{\prime}(x)\rvert\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x+\theta^{\prime}_{\operatorname{sign}v^{-}(L)}(H)v^{-}(L)+\begin{cases}0&\gamma>1,\\
\lambda\lVert v\rVert_{p}&\gamma=1,\\
\infty&\gamma<1;\end{cases}

whereas foru≢0u\not\equiv 0instead we havelimε→0+F​(u+ε​v)−F​(u)ε=∫0L𝒟φ​(u′​(x))​[v′​(x)]​d​x+𝒟θ​(u−​(L)−H)​[v−​(L)]+λ​γ​∥u∥pγ−p​(∫{u≠0}|u​(x)|p−1​sign⁡u​(x)​v​(x)​d​x+δp,1​∫{u=0}|v​(x)|​d​x).\begin{split}&\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0^{+}}\frac{F(u+\varepsilon v)-F(u)}{\varepsilon}\\
&=\int_{0}^{L}\mathcal{D}_{\varphi}\bigl(u^{\prime}(x)\bigr)[v^{\prime}(x)]\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x+\mathcal{D}_{\theta}\bigl(u^{-}(L)-H\bigr)[v^{-}(L)]\\
&\mathrel{\phantom{=}}{}+\lambda\gamma\lVert u\rVert_{p}^{\gamma-p}\left(\int_{\{u\neq 0\}}\lvert u(x)\rvert^{p-1}\operatorname{sign}u(x)v(x)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x+\delta_{p,1}\int_{\{u=0\}}\lvert v(x)\rvert\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\right).\end{split}

## B.1Differentiable setting

Ifφ\varphiandθ\thetaare differentiable everywhere, we can expand out the definition of𝒟φ,𝒟θ\mathcal{D}_{\varphi},\mathcal{D}_{\theta}and obtain the simplified expressionlimε→0+F​(u+ε​v)−F​(u)ε=∫{u′≠0}φ′​(u′​(x))​sign⁡(u′​(x))​v′​(x)​d​x+∫{u′=0}φ′​(0)​|v′​(x)|​d​x+{|v−​(L)|u−​(L)=Hθ′​(|u−​(L)−H|)​sign⁡(u−​(L)−H)​v−​(L)u−​(L)≠H+λ​γ​∥u∥pγ−p​(∫{u≠0}|u​(x)|p−1​sign⁡u​(x)​v​(x)​d​x+δp,1​∫{u=0}|v​(x)|​d​x).\begin{split}&\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0^{+}}\frac{F(u+\varepsilon v)-F(u)}{\varepsilon}\\
&=\int_{\{u^{\prime}\neq 0\}}\varphi^{\prime}\bigl(u^{\prime}(x)\bigr)\operatorname{sign}\bigl(u^{\prime}(x)\bigr)v^{\prime}(x)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\{u^{\prime}=0\}}\varphi^{\prime}(0)\lvert v^{\prime}(x)\rvert\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\\
&\mathrel{\phantom{=}}{}+\begin{cases}\lvert v^{-}(L)\rvert&u^{-}(L)=H\\
\theta^{\prime}\bigl(\lvert u^{-}(L)-H\rvert\bigr)\operatorname{sign}(u^{-}(L)-H)v^{-}(L)&u^{-}(L)\neq H\end{cases}\\
&\mathrel{\phantom{=}}{}+\lambda\gamma\lVert u\rVert_{p}^{\gamma-p}\left(\int_{\{u\neq 0\}}\lvert u(x)\rvert^{p-1}\operatorname{sign}u(x)v(x)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x+\delta_{p,1}\int_{\{u=0\}}\lvert v(x)\rvert\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\right).\end{split}

## B.1.1Special casep=γ=1p=\gamma=1

Sinceu​(x)=∫0xu′​(y)​d​yu(x)=\int_{0}^{x}u^{\prime}(y)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}y, for non-decreasing functions the functional can be rewritten asF​(u)=∫0Lφ​(u′​(x))​d​x+θ​(H−u−​(L))+λ​∫0L∫0xu′​(y)​d​y​d​x=∫0L[φ​(u′​(x))+λ​(L−x)​u′​(x)]​d​x+θ​(H−u−​(L)).\begin{split}F(u)&=\int_{0}^{L}\varphi\bigl(u^{\prime}(x)\bigr)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x+\theta\bigl(H-u^{-}(L)\bigr)+\lambda\int_{0}^{L}\int_{0}^{x}u^{\prime}(y)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}y\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\\
&=\int_{0}^{L}\bigl[\varphi\bigl(u^{\prime}(x)\bigr)+\lambda(L-x)u^{\prime}(x)\bigr]\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x+\theta\bigl(H-u^{-}(L)\bigr).\end{split}

The first integral is convex inu′u^{\prime}, whereas the second addend is concave.

## Optimality conditions

Let us derive simplified optimality conditions obtained from vertical variations.
- •

Ifu−​(L)=Hu^{-}(L)=H, the first variation with respect tov∈C1​((0,L))v\in C^{1}((0,L))withv|(0,x0]=0v\rvert_{(0,x_{0}]}=0islimε→0+F​(u+ε​v)−F​(u)ε=∫x0L[φ′​(u′​(x))​v′​(x)+λ​(L−x)​v′​(x)]​d​x+|v−​(L)|.\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0^{+}}\frac{F(u+\varepsilon v)-F(u)}{\varepsilon}=\int_{x_{0}}^{L}\bigl[\varphi^{\prime}\bigl(u^{\prime}(x)\bigr)v^{\prime}(x)+\lambda(L-x)v^{\prime}(x)\bigr]\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x+\lvert v^{-}(L)\rvert.

Whenv−​(L)≥0v^{-}(L)\geq 0the one-sided variation is∫x0L[φ′​(u′​(x))+λ​(L−x)+1]​v′​(x)​d​x.\int_{x_{0}}^{L}\bigl[\varphi^{\prime}\bigl(u^{\prime}(x)\bigr)+\lambda(L-x)+1\bigr]v^{\prime}(x)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x.

Ifuuis a local minimizer, this must be positive for everyvvsuch that∫x0Lv′​(x)​d​x≥0\int_{x_{0}}^{L}v^{\prime}(x)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\geq 0, henceφ′​(u′​(x))+λ​(L−x)+1\varphi^{\prime}\bigl(u^{\prime}(x)\bigr)+\lambda(L-x)+1must be a
non-negative constant.

Whenv−​(L)≤0v^{-}(L)\leq 0the one-sided variation is∫x0L[φ′​(u′​(x))+λ​(L−x)−1]​v′​(x)​d​x.\int_{x_{0}}^{L}\bigl[\varphi^{\prime}\bigl(u^{\prime}(x)\bigr)+\lambda(L-x)-1\bigr]v^{\prime}(x)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x.

Ifuuis a local minimizer, this must be positive for everyvvsuch that∫x0Lv′​(x)​d​x≤0\int_{x_{0}}^{L}v^{\prime}(x)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\leq 0, henceφ′​(u′​(x))+λ​(L−x)−1\varphi^{\prime}\bigl(u^{\prime}(x)\bigr)+\lambda(L-x)-1must be a
non-positive constant.

Putting these two conditions together we get thatφ′​(u′​(x))+λ​(L−x)\varphi^{\prime}\bigl(u^{\prime}(x)\bigr)+\lambda(L-x)must be a constant in[0,1][0,1].
- •

If insteadu−​(L)<Hu^{-}(L)<H, then for every variationv∈C1​((0,L))v\in C^{1}\bigl((0,L)\bigr)withv|(0,x0]=0v\rvert_{(0,x_{0}]}=0we havelimε→0F​(u+ε​v)−F​(u)ε=∫x0L[φ′​(u′​(x))​v′​(x)+λ​(L−x)​v′​(x)]​d​x−θ′​(H−u−​(L))​v−​(L)=∫x0L[φ′​(u′​(x))+λ​(L−x)−θ′​(H−u−​(L))]​v′​(x)​d​x.\begin{split}\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\frac{F(u+\varepsilon v)-F(u)}{\varepsilon}&=\int_{x_{0}}^{L}\bigl[\varphi^{\prime}\bigl(u^{\prime}(x)\bigr)v^{\prime}(x)+\lambda(L-x)v^{\prime}(x)\bigr]\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x-\theta^{\prime}\bigl(H-u^{-}(L)\bigr)v^{-}(L)\\
&=\int_{x_{0}}^{L}\bigl[\varphi^{\prime}\bigl(u^{\prime}(x)\bigr)+\lambda(L-x)-\theta^{\prime}\bigl(H-u^{-}(L)\bigr)\bigr]v^{\prime}(x)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x.\end{split}

Ifuuis a local minimizer, then this variation must be zero, hence by the arbitrariness ofvvwe deduceφ′​(u′​(x))+λ​(L−x)=θ′​(H−u−​(L))for a.e.x∈(x0,L).\varphi^{\prime}\bigl(u^{\prime}(x)\bigr)+\lambda(L-x)=\theta^{\prime}\bigl(H-u^{-}(L)\bigr)\qquad\text{for a.e.\ $x\in(x_{0},L)$}.(B.2)

Under continuity assumptions, evaluating atx=Lx=Lwe getφ′​(u′​(L))=θ′​(H−u−​(L)).\varphi^{\prime}\bigl(u^{\prime}(L)\bigr)=\theta^{\prime}\bigl(H-u^{-}(L)\bigr).

We may also consider horizontal variations arising from translations ofuu.
Givenα∈ℝ\alpha\in\mathbb{R}, define the extended functionuα​(x)={0x≤0,u​(x)x∈(0,L)u−​(L)+α​(x−L),x≥L,u_{\alpha}(x)=\begin{cases}0&x\leq 0,\\
u(x)&x\in(0,L)\\
u^{-}(L)+\alpha(x-L),&x\geq L,\end{cases}

and observe thatF​(u)=F​(uα)F(u)=F(u_{\alpha}).
- •

Right translation.Sinceu′​(L)≥0u^{\prime}(L)\geq 0, we havelimτ→0+F(uα(⋅−τ))−F(uα)τ=−φ​(u′​(L))+θ′​(H−u−​(L))​u′​(L)−λ​u−​(L)≥0.\lim_{\tau\to 0^{+}}\frac{F(u_{\alpha}(\,\cdot\,-\tau))-F(u_{\alpha})}{\tau}=-\varphi\bigl(u^{\prime}(L)\bigr)+\theta^{\prime}\bigl(H-u^{-}(L)\bigr)u^{\prime}(L)-\lambda u^{-}(L)\geq 0.
- •

Left translation.The left translation is admissible if and only ifuuis
identically zero in a right neighborhood of0.
Ifu−​(L)<Hu^{-}(L)<H, we havelimτ→0+F(uα(⋅+τ))−F(uα)τ=φ​(α)−θ′​(H−u−​(L))​α+λ​u−​(L)≥0,\lim_{\tau\to 0^{+}}\frac{F(u_{\alpha}(\,\cdot\,+\tau))-F(u_{\alpha})}{\tau}=\varphi(\alpha)-\theta^{\prime}\bigl(H-u^{-}(L)\bigr)\alpha+\lambda u^{-}(L)\geq 0,

from which we deduce the necessary conditionλ​u−​(L)≥supα∈ℝ(θ′​(H−u−​(L))​α−φ​(α))=φ∗​(θ′​(H−u−​(L)))=φ∗​(φ′​(u′​(L))).\lambda u^{-}(L)\geq\sup_{\alpha\in\mathbb{R}}\bigl(\theta^{\prime}\bigl(H-u^{-}(L)\bigr)\alpha-\varphi(\alpha)\bigr)=\varphi^{*}\bigl(\theta^{\prime}\bigl(H-u^{-}(L)\bigr)\bigr)=\varphi^{*}\bigl(\varphi^{\prime}\bigl(u^{\prime}(L)\bigr)\bigr).

If insteadu−​(L)=Hu^{-}(L)=H, thenlimτ→0+F(uα(⋅+τ))−F(uα)τ=φ​(α)+θ′​(H−u−​(L))​|α|+λ​u−​(L)≥0,\lim_{\tau\to 0^{+}}\frac{F(u_{\alpha}(\,\cdot\,+\tau))-F(u_{\alpha})}{\tau}=\varphi(\alpha)+\theta^{\prime}\bigl(H-u^{-}(L)\bigr)\lvert\alpha\rvert+\lambda u^{-}(L)\geq 0,

which is a condition always satisfied since the three summands are individually non-negative.

Recall that the minimizer is a convex functionu∈W1,1​((0,L);[0,H])u\in W^{1,1}\bigl((0,L);[0,H]\bigr)withu+​(0)=0u^{+}(0)=0.
Letx0=max{x∈[0,L]:u|(0,x]=0}x_{0}=\max\{x\in[0,L]:u\rvert_{(0,x]}=0\}.
From (B.2) we deduce that the optimal profileuumust solve the ODEφ′​(u′​(x))+λ​(L−x)=θ′​(H−u−​(L))≕C,\varphi^{\prime}\bigl(u^{\prime}(x)\bigr)+\lambda(L-x)=\theta^{\prime}\bigl(H-u^{-}(L)\bigr)\eqqcolon C,(B.3)

whereCCis a constant.
From the inequalitiesφ′​(0)≤φ′≤φ′​(∞)=1\varphi^{\prime}(0)\leq\varphi^{\prime}\leq\varphi^{\prime}(\infty)=1andθ′​(∞)≤θ′≤θ′​(0)=1\theta^{\prime}(\infty)\leq\theta^{\prime}\leq\theta^{\prime}(0)=1,
evaluating (B.3) atx=Lx=Lwe deducemax⁡{φ′​(0),θ′​(∞)}≤C≤1\max\{\varphi^{\prime}(0),\theta^{\prime}(\infty)\}\leq C\leq 1;
evaluating (B.3) atx=x0x=x_{0}we deduceλ​(L−x0)≤1−φ′​(0)\lambda(L-x_{0})\leq 1-\varphi^{\prime}(0),
that isx0≥L−1−φ′​(0)λ.x_{0}\geq L-\frac{1-\varphi^{\prime}(0)}{\lambda}.

Ifλ​L>1−φ′​(0)\lambda L>1-\varphi^{\prime}(0), this ensuresx0>0x_{0}>0.171717Incidentally, observe that in then linear caseφ​(t)=t\varphi(t)=twe haveλ​(L−x0)≤1−1=0\lambda(L-x_{0})\leq 1-1=0, thereforex0=Lx_{0}=Land the optimal profile cannot detach from0.
This is consistent with the results inSubsection 3.2.

We now wish to find a condition relatingx0x_{0}andCC. To do so, we perform yet another variation in
the following manner. Assumingx0∈(0,L)x_{0}\in(0,L), fix an arbitrary pointx¯∈(x0,L)\bar{x}\in(x_{0},L)and,

u(x)&x\in[\bar{x},L).\end{cases}

A straightforward computation shows that0=dd​ε|ε=0​F​(uε)=∫x0x¯{φ′​(u′​(x))​[u′​(x)+u′′​(x)​(x−x¯)]+λ​u​(x)}​d​x,\begin{split}0&=\frac{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}}{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\varepsilon}\biggr\rvert_{\varepsilon=0}F(u_{\varepsilon})=\int_{x_{0}}^{\bar{x}}\left\{\varphi^{\prime}\bigl(u^{\prime}(x)\bigr)\bigl[u^{\prime}(x)+u^{\prime\prime}(x)(x-\bar{x})\bigr]+\lambda u(x)\right\}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x,\end{split}

from which, dividing byx¯−x0\bar{x}-x_{0}and sendingx¯→x0+\bar{x}\to x_{0}^{+}, we infer0=φ′​(u′​(x0))​u′​(x0)+λ​u​(x0)=φ′​(u′​(x0))​u′​(x0).0=\varphi^{\prime}\bigl(u^{\prime}(x_{0})\bigr)u^{\prime}(x_{0})+\lambda u(x_{0})=\varphi^{\prime}\bigl(u^{\prime}(x_{0})\bigr)u^{\prime}(x_{0}).

and consequently (B.3) becomesφ′​(u′​(x))=φ′​(0)+λ​(x−x0).\varphi^{\prime}\bigl(u^{\prime}(x)\bigr)=\varphi^{\prime}(0)+\lambda(x-x_{0}).(B.4)

## Explicit formula for the optimal profile

Recall the Fenchel–Young inequalityφ​(t)+φ∗​(τ)≥τ​t\varphi(t)+\varphi^{*}(\tau)\geq\tau twith its equality conditionsφ​(t)+φ∗​(τ)=t​τ⟺τ∈∂φ​(t)⟺t=∂φ∗​(τ)\varphi(t)+\varphi^{*}(\tau)=t\tau\qquad\Longleftrightarrow\qquad\tau\in\partial\varphi(t)\qquad\Longleftrightarrow\qquad t=\partial\varphi^{*}(\tau)(B.5)

expressed in terms of the sub-differential. Even whenφ\varphiis not strictly convex, the ODE
(B.4) impliesu′​(x)∈∂φ∗​(φ′​(0)+λ​(x−x0))u^{\prime}(x)\in\partial\varphi^{*}\bigl(\varphi^{\prime}(0)+\lambda(x-x_{0})\bigr).
Sinceφ∗\varphi^{*}is locally Lipschitz in its domain, by Rademacher’s theorem it is differentiable
almost everywhere,181818Actually, being one-dimensional, it is differentiable outside of a countable set.thereforeu′​(x)=(φ∗)′​(φ′​(0)+λ​(x−x0))for almost everyx∈(x0,L).u^{\prime}(x)=(\varphi^{*})^{\prime}\bigl(\varphi^{\prime}(0)+\lambda(x-x_{0})\bigr)\qquad\text{for almost every $x\in(x_{0},L)$}.(B.6)

This is sufficient to infer thatu​(x)=∫x0x(φ∗)′​(φ′​(0)+λ​(y−x0))​d​y=1λ​∫φ′​(0)φ′​(0)+λ​(x−x0)(φ∗)′​(z)​d​z=1λ​[φ∗​(φ′​(0)+λ​(x−x0))−φ∗​(φ′​(0))]=1λ​φ∗​(φ′​(0)+λ​(x−x0)).\begin{split}u(x)&=\int_{x_{0}}^{x}(\varphi^{*})^{\prime}\bigl(\varphi^{\prime}(0)+\lambda(y-x_{0})\bigr)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}y=\frac{1}{\lambda}\int_{\varphi^{\prime}(0)}^{\varphi^{\prime}(0)+\lambda(x-x_{0})}(\varphi^{*})^{\prime}(z)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}z\\
&=\frac{1}{\lambda}\bigl[\varphi^{*}\bigl(\varphi^{\prime}(0)+\lambda(x-x_{0})\bigr)-\varphi^{*}\bigl(\varphi^{\prime}(0)\bigr)\bigr]=\frac{1}{\lambda}\varphi^{*}\bigl(\varphi^{\prime}(0)+\lambda(x-x_{0})\bigr).\end{split}(B.7)

## Formula for the resulting functional

Settingτ=φ′​(t)\tau=\varphi^{\prime}(t)in (B.5) shows thatφ​(t)=t​φ′​(t)−φ∗​(φ′​(t)).\varphi(t)=t\varphi^{\prime}(t)-\varphi^{*}\bigl(\varphi^{\prime}(t)\bigr).

Evaluating it att=u′​(x)t=u^{\prime}(x)and using (B.4) and
(B.7) leads toφ​(u′​(x))+λ​u​(x)=u′​(x)​φ′​(u′​(x))−φ∗​(φ′​(u′​(x)))+φ∗​(φ′​(0)+λ​(x−x0))=u′​(x)​(φ′​(0)+λ​(x−x0)).\begin{split}\varphi\bigl(u^{\prime}(x)\bigr)+\lambda u(x)&=u^{\prime}(x)\varphi^{\prime}\bigl(u^{\prime}(x)\bigr)-\varphi^{*}\bigl(\varphi^{\prime}\bigl(u^{\prime}(x)\bigr)\bigr)+\varphi^{*}\bigl(\varphi^{\prime}(0)+\lambda(x-x_{0})\bigr)\\
&=u^{\prime}(x)\bigl(\varphi^{\prime}(0)+\lambda(x-x_{0})\bigr).\end{split}

Using (B.6), we get∫x0L{φ​(u′​(x))+λ​u​(x)}​d​x=∫x0L(φ′​(0)+λ​(x−x0))​(φ∗)′​(φ′​(0)+λ​(x−x0))​d​x=1λ​∫φ′​(0)φ′​(0)+λ​(L−x0)y​(φ∗)′​(y)​d​y,\begin{split}\int_{x_{0}}^{L}\left\{\varphi\bigl(u^{\prime}(x)\bigr)+\lambda u(x)\right\}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x&=\int_{x_{0}}^{L}\bigl(\varphi^{\prime}(0)+\lambda(x-x_{0})\bigr)(\varphi^{*})^{\prime}\bigl(\varphi^{\prime}(0)+\lambda(x-x_{0})\bigr)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\\
&=\frac{1}{\lambda}\int_{\varphi^{\prime}(0)}^{\varphi^{\prime}(0)+\lambda(L-x_{0})}y(\varphi^{*})^{\prime}(y)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}y,\end{split}

hence the functional becomesF​(u)=1λ​∫φ′​(0)φ′​(0)+λ​(L−x0)y​(φ∗)′​(y)​d​y+θ​(|H−u​(L)|),F(u)=\frac{1}{\lambda}\int_{\varphi^{\prime}(0)}^{\varphi^{\prime}(0)+\lambda(L-x_{0})}y(\varphi^{*})^{\prime}(y)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}y+\theta\bigl(\lvert H-u(L)\rvert\bigr),(B.8)

which has to be minimized overx0∈[L−1−φ′​(0)λ,L]x_{0}\in\left[L-\frac{1-\varphi^{\prime}(0)}{\lambda},L\right].
In addition, we know that the minimizer satisfiesu​(L)≤Hu(L)\leq H;
therefore, let us find a more explicit condition onx0x_{0}which ensures this property and allows to
remove the absolute value in the argument ofθ\theta.

We claim thatφ∗:[φ′​(0),1]→[0,φ∗​(1)]\varphi^{*}:[\varphi^{\prime}(0),1]\to[0,\varphi^{*}(1)]is strictly increasing, continuous and bijective.
Suppose thatφ∗​(τ)=0\varphi^{*}(\tau)=0for someτ∈[φ′​(0),1]\tau\in[\varphi^{\prime}(0),1]; thenφ​(t)−φ​(0)=φ​(t)=φ​(t)+φ∗​(τ)≥τ​t,\varphi(t)-\varphi(0)=\varphi(t)=\varphi(t)+\varphi^{*}(\tau)\geq\tau t,

from which, dividing byttand lettingt→0+t\to 0^{+}, we getφ′​(0)≥τ\varphi^{\prime}(0)\geq\tau, henceτ=φ′​(0)\tau=\varphi^{\prime}(0).
This proves thatτ>φ′​(0)\tau>\varphi^{\prime}(0)impliesφ∗​(τ)>0\varphi^{*}(\tau)>0.
Sinceφ∗\varphi^{*}is convex, for everyσ>τ\sigma>\tauwe haveφ∗​(σ)≥φ∗​(0)+στ​(φ∗​(τ)−φ∗​(0))=στ​φ∗​(τ)>φ∗​(τ),\varphi^{*}(\sigma)\geq\varphi^{*}(0)+\frac{\sigma}{\tau}\bigl(\varphi^{*}(\tau)-\varphi^{*}(0)\bigr)=\frac{\sigma}{\tau}\varphi^{*}(\tau)>\varphi^{*}(\tau),

hence the claimed monotonicity. The continuity follows from the basic properties of the Legendre transform.
The bijectivety follows from continuity and strict monotonicity.

Using (B.7), the conditionu​(L)≤Hu(L)\leq His therefore trivially
satisfied ifλ​H≥φ∗​(1)\lambda H\geq\varphi^{*}(1), otherwise it is equivalent toφ′​(0)+λ​(L−x0)≤(φ∗)−1​(λ​H)\varphi^{\prime}(0)+\lambda(L-x_{0})\leq(\varphi^{*})^{-1}(\lambda H), that isx0≥L−(φ∗)−1​(λ​H)−φ′​(0)λ,x_{0}\geq L-\frac{(\varphi^{*})^{-1}(\lambda H)-\varphi^{\prime}(0)}{\lambda},

where by(φ∗)−1(\varphi^{*})^{-1}we denote the inverse of the strictly increasing restrictionφ∗|[φ′​(0),1]\varphi^{*}\rvert_{[\varphi^{\prime}(0),1]}. This latter inequality is correct in both cases if we interpret(φ∗)−1​(λ​H)=1(\varphi^{*})^{-1}(\lambda H)=1whenλ​H≥φ∗​(1)\lambda H\geq\varphi^{*}(1).
Notice that, since(φ∗)−1​(λ​H)≤1(\varphi^{*})^{-1}(\lambda H)\leq 1, this condition onx0x_{0}is more restrictive
than the previously found lower boundx0≥L−1−φ′​(0)λx_{0}\geq L-\frac{1-\varphi^{\prime}(0)}{\lambda}.

## Reduction to a one-dimensional minimization problem

Finding the optimal profileuuhas been reduced to minimizing the functionf(x0)≔1λ∫φ′​(0)φ′​(0)+λ​(L−x0)y(φ∗)′(y)dy+θ(H−1λφ∗(φ′(0)+λ(L−x0)))f(x_{0})\coloneqq\frac{1}{\lambda}\int_{\varphi^{\prime}(0)}^{\varphi^{\prime}(0)+\lambda(L-x_{0})}y(\varphi^{*})^{\prime}(y)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}y+\theta\mathopen{}\left(H-\frac{1}{\lambda}\varphi^{*}\bigl(\varphi^{\prime}(0)+\lambda(L-x_{0})\bigr)\right)(B.9)

under the constraintL−(φ∗)−1​(λ​H)−φ′​(0)λ≤x0≤L.L-\frac{(\varphi^{*})^{-1}(\lambda H)-\varphi^{\prime}(0)}{\lambda}\leq x_{0}\leq L.(B.10)

Ifφ\varphiis strictly convex thenφ∗\varphi^{*}is differentiable everywhere, therefore we can computed​F​(u)d​x0=f′​(x0)=−y​(φ∗)′​(y)|y=φ′​(0)+λ​(L−x0)+θ′​(H−φ∗​(y)/λ)​(φ∗)′​(y)|y=φ′​(0)+λ​(L−x0)=(φ∗)′​(y)​[θ′​(H−φ∗​(y)/λ)−y]|y=φ′​(0)+λ​(L−x0).\begin{split}\frac{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}F(u)}{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x_{0}}&=f^{\prime}(x_{0})=-y(\varphi^{*})^{\prime}(y)\bigr\rvert_{y=\varphi^{\prime}(0)+\lambda(L-x_{0})}+\theta^{\prime}\bigl(H-\varphi^{*}(y)/\lambda\bigr)(\varphi^{*})^{\prime}(y)\bigr\rvert_{y=\varphi^{\prime}(0)+\lambda(L-x_{0})}\\
&=(\varphi^{*})^{\prime}(y)\bigl[\theta^{\prime}\bigl(H-\varphi^{*}(y)/\lambda\bigr)-y\bigr]\bigr\rvert_{y=\varphi^{\prime}(0)+\lambda(L-x_{0})}.\end{split}

If the minimizerx0x_{0}is an interior point, it must satisfyf′​(x0)=0f^{\prime}(x_{0})=0, which is equivalent toθ′(H−1λφ∗(φ′(0)+λ(L−x0)))=φ′(0)+λ(L−x0),\theta^{\prime}\mathopen{}\left(H-\frac{1}{\lambda}\varphi^{*}\bigl(\varphi^{\prime}(0)+\lambda(L-x_{0})\bigr)\right)=\varphi^{\prime}(0)+\lambda(L-x_{0}),

because(φ∗)′​(φ′​(0)+λ​(L−x0))≥φ∗​(φ′​(0)+λ​(L−x0))−φ∗​(φ′​(0))λ​(L−x0)>0(\varphi^{*})^{\prime}\bigl(\varphi^{\prime}(0)+\lambda(L-x_{0})\bigr)\geq\frac{\varphi^{*}\bigl(\varphi^{\prime}(0)+\lambda(L-x_{0})\bigr)-\varphi^{*}\bigl(\varphi^{\prime}(0)\bigr)}{\lambda(L-x_{0})}>0

cannot vanish by the strict monotonicity ofφ∗\varphi^{*}.
